# AUDIT 2026-10-01 - ORDER SM-AUD1

Full functional audit of the Abelard production host (basilic). **Read-only: a measurement, not a repair.** Nothing was fixed in-flight.

## Read this first

Measured on basilic on 2026-10-01 between about 17:00 and 22:00 EDT. 140 rows across nine sections.

**Three things changed on the host while the audit ran.** Read the rows with them in mind.

- **16:31-16:32** - order SM-JAN2 J1 (date sanity) was deployed at `c3b7dbb` and its repair applied. Sections A, B, C and I were measured across that moment; D to H after it.
- **21:19** - one line was fixed, authorized by name: `"unmapped"` added to `_CLUSTER_BUY_COLS` (`56e904f`). Row D34 and the note under A8 describe `/clusters.csv?which=buy` answering HTTP 500, which was true when measured and is not true now.
- **22:11** - a documents-only commit, `005a0f0` (SM-DATA1). Row H1 records live HEAD as `c3b7dbb`; it is now `005a0f0`, and still equal to `origin/main`.

One piece of housekeeping: an auditor copied Tailscale's GUI cache into `~/probes/aud1/` to read it, and that copy held private node key material. The copy was deleted at 22:14. No probe file holds a key value.

## What matters most

1. **The outage has no established cause (B3 UNKNOWN), and could recur today (B5 RED).** What is established overturns the earlier account at both ends. The tailnet address was already gone at 01:31:55 on 09-04, in the first boot, with the owner logged in and *before* macOS 26.6.2 was installed at 01:33. And the Tailscale backend recovered by 22:20:35 on 09-19, before the display woke and 59 seconds before the console unlock. Key expiry, the App Store build, extension re-approval and a lost login item are each ruled out with evidence. The one untested lead is NordVPN: 10.6.1 auto-installed at 01:28:38, the only software change before the first failing boot, and its Threat Protection transparent proxy diverted flows throughout the outage. It is a lead, not a cause - the same proxy ran for nine days after recovery while Tailscale worked. The log that would decide it, the Tailscale extension's own for 09-04 to 09-19, no longer exists on the host.
2. **The machine is exposed.** A restart-requiring macOS update is pending with automatic install on. FileVault is on with no auto-login, so a power cut stops at the disk-unlock screen and nothing runs. All 15 jobs are per-user LaunchAgents. Host DNS now runs through Tailscale's forwarder, so an extension fault also breaks name resolution - which is what the eight-hour failure on 09-23 was.
3. **Nothing was lost to the outage.** Every scheduled job ran every night of it, and the universal Form 4 corpus has no hole (C2): the nine business days 09-08 to 09-18 were ingested live. The backfill that was owed is not needed.
4. **No arrival ledger exists (I1, I2 RED), and the Q3 window opens about 11-03.** The 13F leg reads only form `13F-HR`. Pershing Square's Q2 book is on EDGAR under a CIK the roster does not track, its old CIK filed a 13F-NT, and the nightly scan reports that filer OK. The live opposed-pairs board already mixes quarters on 12 of 80 rows.
5. **Seven views print or export something a reader cannot use (D RED).** Four PDFs run their identifying columns off the page; a committee drill-down prints and exports the all-committees roll.
6. **Display defects in data quality (E RED).** Placeholder tickers still render as `NONE` and `[NONE]` on two boards. 1,495 Senate rows are repeats of a row in another filing. `market_cap` covers 1 of the top 10 issuers on the pressure board.
7. **The Open WebUI lane has been down since 09-04 (A12 RED)** while launchd shows it running.

## Found the same day, outside the audit

While writing the data dictionary (SM-DATA1), a parser defect was found and then confirmed against raw EDGAR XML: **the Form 4 parser reads the 10b5-1 and role checkboxes only when written `1`** (`form4.py:116-123`). Filings that write `true` are stored as `plan_flag = 0` with `role` NULL. Sundar Pichai's GOOGL filing `0001193125-26-114850` says `aff10b5One=true`, `isDirector=true`, `isOfficer=true`; the database holds `plan_flag = 0`, `role` NULL. 106,366 rows in 51,063 filings carry `plan_flag = 0` with `role` NULL - the ceiling on what is affected. Among sales, the plan share is 61.7% where the role was read and 20.0% where it is NULL. Every board that filters on "discretionary" inherits this. It is not an audit row because no section asked for it; it should become an order.

## Method

Nine sections. Each was measured by one auditor from the logs, the code on disk and the databases (opened read-only), then re-run by an **independent verifier** who was told to distrust every GREEN. The RAG shown is the verifier's; a row the verifier changed says so. GREEN = verified working with evidence. AMBER = working but degraded, stale, partly verified or carrying a caveat. RED = broken, missing or false. UNKNOWN = could not be established, with the reason.

## Every RED and UNKNOWN

- **A12 RED** - com.abelard.qwen-ui - Open WebUI via Colima plus socat bridge on Tailscale :3000 (KeepAlive). launchd shows this job as running, but the UI is down: only the socat bridge is alive and its backend refuses connections. Colima has failed to start on every attempt logged since the 09-04 reboot, and the container has not run since.
- **B3 UNKNOWN** - THE CAUSE - why Tailscale stayed disconnected for two weeks and then came back. Not established. Candidates 1-4 are each ruled out by evidence: the Tailscale client was running with its identity loaded and WantRunning=true from 01:37:49, yet never completed a control-plane login until a human used the console on 09-19. Best-supported hypothesis: the boot-time login wedged in the auto-locked post-update session, but the same unlock on 09-04 01:59 did not release it, so the mechanism is unproven and the item stays open.
- **B5 RED** - Could it recur TODAY - is the machine in the state that caused it. Yes. Every ingredient of 09-04 is unchanged and a restart-requiring macOS update is already pending with automatic install on, so the next console unlock can arm another overnight reboot. A power cut would be worse: with FileVault on and no auto-login the machine stops at the disk-unlock screen and nothing runs at all.

Only filed-with-rows is represented honestly: filed-empty survives as an orphan accession with no period, and awaited and NT-notice cannot be represented at all. Nothing reads 13F-NT or 13F-HR/A, so Pershing Square's Q2 13F-NT (filed 2026-08-14) is invisible while the nightly scan reports that filer OK.
- **I5 RED** - What opposed-pairs / convergence need from an arrival ledger to be honest during a filing window; how they pick the newest period per filer. Each filer's newest period is simply the latest period that has rows in thirteenf_holdings, and the prior is the next one down, adjacent or not. The live board already mixes quarters on 12 of 80 rows and does not show the period per side; a replay of the Q2 window shows 27-28% mixed rows and about half the mid-window board gone by deadline day. Convergence groups by (ticker, period) with no statement of how many filers have reported.

## A - Schedule health

Section A has 18 rows: 6 GREEN, 11 AMBER, 1 RED, 0 UNKNOWN. Nothing was changed on the host; all history below is read from logs and from the databases opened mode=ro.

**The four questions**
1. **HOLX:** cleared. The last HOLX vendor_error was in the 09-21 21:00 run; the nightly leg has exited 0 on HOLX since the 09-22 21:00 run. The prices job as a whole kept exiting 1 for other reasons through 09-26 (dividend and split disagreements on PRU, SPGI and TMUS; the 09-23 session undelivered by both vendors for two nights). Its first fully clean night was 09-27, and 09-27 to 09-30 are four clean nights in a row.
2. **Options leg:** the last 7 passes (09-24 to 09-30) each recorded tickers=32, ok=27, gaps=0, no_chain=5, dropped=0, with 9,737 to 10,802 contracts. The same five tickers (ADGI, CLBR, CMMB, CTRA, GUTS) have been chainless on all 55 passes since 08-07, and the scan.log line does not print no_chain. Whether CTRA and GUTS truly have no listed options is not established.
3. **Dev checkout:** no plist names ~/Code/Abelard, and every live venv resolves its code inside abelard-live. The dev checkout still holds production state: consensus/data is a symlink into it (22 GB, including both consensus jobs' launchd logs).
4. **fdu:** healthy (7 of 7 runs rc=0), still running from ~/Code/Abelard-fdu on a branch 20 commits off main. Main has no fdu/ directory, and the exception and its 2026-10-21 expiry are not written anywhere in the live tree.

**The one RED**
- **qwen-ui (A12):** launchd shows it running, but Open WebUI is down. Only the socat bridge is alive; Colima has failed on every start attempt since the 09-04 reboot and the backend refuses connections.

**Other AMBER findings worth an order**
- **Consensus collector (A15):** 395 of its last 400 passes report degraded (gamma /events pagination 422, data-api 429s, poll budget 500 against about 31,000 markets due).
- **Consensus dossier (A16):** all 60 logged runs report degraded, so the status carries no signal.
- **Queue digest (A9):** 204 alerts pending and growing, oldest 77.9 days, with the consumer disabled.
- **Chain timing (A18):** the scan-to-brief order rests on a 4-7 minute clock margin; the scan overran 23:15 on 09-04.
- **Host network (A18):** outbound network was lost for part of 09-23, roughly 11:00 to 17:00.
- **qwen-serve (A11):** serving, but inference was not exercised by this audit.

**GREEN:** smart-money scan, brief, efd-probe, capex scan, capex dashboard and smart-money dashboard. The scan's exit code is insensitive (it exits 0 unless all sources fail), so its GREEN rests on the per-leg [OK] lines.

**Disabled by design:** morning-briefs and queue.consumer are installed, disabled and not loaded.

**Side effects of the audit, disclosed:** probe scripts were written only to ~/probes/aud1/. Read-only HTTP GETs to the services made them append their own access lines: the smart-money dashboard log, two lines in qwen-serve.out.log, and two "Connection refused" lines in qwen-ui.err.log. No database, plist, checkout or launchd state was touched.

Local copies of the probe scripts are in C:\Users\mdiba\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-mdiba-OneDrive-Code-Polymarket\41c384ae-7dc3-4e08-9bd8-17eec9ae9009\scratchpad\ (a1_plists.sh through a16_universe.py).

### A - evidence

**A12 - RED - com.abelard.qwen-ui - Open WebUI via Colima plus socat bridge on Tailscale :3000 (KeepAlive)**

- Evidence: `launchctl list`: `69743 1 com.abelard.qwen-ui`; `launchctl print`: state = running, runs = 10577, last exit code = 1. `ps`: `69743 Sat Sep 19 22:19:43 2026 /opt/homebrew/bin/socat TCP-LISTEN:3000,bind=100.106.84.115,fork,reuseaddr TCP:127.0.0.1:3000`. `curl http://100.106.84.115:3000/` -> `000`; `curl http://127.0.0.1:3000/` -> `000`; qwen-ui.err.log: `2026/10/01 15:16:53 socat[13000] E TCP:127.0.0.1:3000: Connection refused`. `PATH=/opt/homebrew/bin:$PATH colima status` -> `level=fatal msg="colima is not running"`; `limactl list` -> `No instance found`; `ls ~/.colima/default/docker.sock` -> No such file or directory. qwen-ui.out.log, last start attempt: `time="2026-09-19T22:19:45-04:00" level=fatal msg="errors inspecting instance: [vz driver is running but host agent is not]"` / `failed to start containers: open-webui`. First such fatal: line 111, `2026-09-04T01:37:52-04:00`; 21154 fatal lines in total. socat bind failures `Can't assign requested address` run from `2026/09/04 01:31:55` to `2026/09/19 22:19:43`.
- Verifier agrees: Confirmed. The only process is pid 69743 socat (since Sep 19 22:19:43) listening on 100.106.84.115:3000; curl to 100.106.84.115:3000 and 127.0.0.1:3000 both return 000 and qwen-ui.err.log gains `socat ... E TCP:127.0.0.1:3000: Connection refused`. `colima status` -> `colima is not running`; `limactl list` -> no instance; ~/.colima/default holds only colima.yaml (no docker.sock); no colima, lima or docker process exists. qwen-ui.out.log: 21,154 `level=fatal` lines, first at line 111 (2026-09-04T01:37:52 `vz driver is running but host agent is not`), last 2026-09-19T22:19:45, then `failed to start containers: open-webui`. run_qwen_ui.sh runs `colima start` and `docker start ... \|\| true` and then execs socat regardless, which is why launchd reports state = running (runs = 10577, last exit code = 1) with nothing behind the port.
- Proposed order: Repair the Colima instance (vz driver up, host agent not) and bring open-webui back; make run_qwen_ui.sh exit non-zero when colima or the container fails, so the job cannot show running with nothing behind the port.

I closed the gap the auditor left open: one POST /api/generate to qwen3:8b (num_predict 12, keep_alive 0) returned http 200 in 5.8 s, response `OK`, done_reason stop, load 5.4 s; /api/ps was empty afterwards and memory free stayed at 88%. So inference works on the 8b model. It stays AMBER for a different reason: 6 of the job's last 7 launchd runs were bind-failure exits, the service was down 09-04..09-19 with nothing noticing, run_qwen_serve.sh still has no wait-for-address guard, and qwen3-coder:30b (18.5 GB) was deliberately not loaded.
- Proposed order: Run one deliberate inference drill against qwen3:8b; make the runner wait for the Tailscale address (as run_qwen_ui.sh does) instead of crash-looping every 10 s when it is absent.

Margins, last 7 nights (ET): prices ends 21:26-21:30, 60+ min before the scan; scan ends 23:10:39, 23:10:27, 23:10:26, 23:08:31, 23:08:03, 23:10:35, 23:10:12; brief PDFs written 23:16:33 at the earliest. Overrun: `2026-09-05T02:30:02Z -> 2026-09-05T03:15:31Z exit 0`, i.e. the 09-04 scan ended 23:15:31 ET, after the brief's 23:15:00 slot. run_brief.sh has no check that the scan finished; it starts with `waf_probe --rider` (default `--max-jitter 1800`, a random 0-30 min sleep). 09-23 outage: efd_probe_log `2026-09-23T12:15:49 no_network ... host offline` and `2026-09-23T15:13:15 no_network`; collector.launchd.err has 5738 name-resolution or retry-failure lines stamped 2026-09-23 11:00-16:59; one collector pass ran `09-23 11:16 -> 09-23 12:51 degraded`; the 18:11 probe was ok.

One run per night 09-24..09-30 in every runner log (see A1, A2, A4, A6, A9, A10, A16) and 6 probe slots a day. Two corrections. (1) The scan has ended after 23:15 three times in scan.log, not once: 08-04 (23:48:11), 08-19 (23:16:26), 09-04 (23:15:31), and 31 runs ended between 23:10 and 23:15. On 08-04 the brief PDF was written at 23:39:05, nine minutes before that scan finished, so the race the row describes as a risk has already happened once; on 08-19 and 09-04 the rider's random sleep saved it (PDFs at 23:20:11 and 23:31:54). (2) The 09-23 outage was longer than `about 11:00 to 17:00`: collector.launchd.err has resolution and retry failures in every hour from 10 to 18 (597 in hour 10, 240 in hour 17, 34 in hour 18; first at 10:06:15), and four collector passes starting 10:02, 11:16, 12:54 and 15:18 ran 71, 95, 140 and 172 minutes. With the 09:12 and 18:11 probes ok, the window is roughly 10:05 to 18:10. The hours 11-16 sum to 5,738, which is the auditor's figure.
- Proposed order: Gate the brief on the scan's completion (wait for the `<<< exit` line or a done-marker) instead of the clock; find out what took the host's network down on 09-23 and whether the INFRA-B1 assertion bundle would have caught it.

### A - found by the verifier, missed by the auditor

- The audit straddles a production deploy. abelard-live was fast-forwarded d135a1d -> c3b7dbb at 16:31:33 today (17 smart_money_daemon files, including scan.py, brief.py, db.py, dashboard.py, form4.py and the house, efd and oge ingest), post_deploy.sh migrated the database, a date repair ran (pre_date_sanity_20261001-163159.db backup and repairs/date_sanity_20261001-163159 at 16:32), and the dashboard was restarted. The auditor's rows cite HEAD d135a1d and dash pid 10944. Every 7-run history for the scan, brief and dashboard describes the previous code; tonight's 22:30 scan and 23:15 brief are the first scheduled runs on c3b7dbb and are unverified.
- Smart-money dashboard: /clusters.csv and /clusters.csv?which=buy return HTTP 500 (`ValueError: CSV contract violation: ['unmapped'] present on a row but neither exported nor listed in omit`), 29 of 29 requests since the restart. The auditor tested only `/`.
- Price store freshness under the prices job: the equity store is one session behind at every 21:00 run (latest held session 2026-09-29 after the 09-30 run; each session's rows arrive on the following night's run). The nightly lag check is relative to the latest held session, so the four `worst exit 0` nights do not show the store was current.
- The 450/451 price holes of 09-23 and 09-24 were the 2026-09-22 session, not the 09-23 session named in the row and its proposed order.
- Queue digest content: the daily file lists only the 40 oldest items per source and is identical day to day apart from date, counts and ages, so new alerts never appear in the file that is described as the delivery.
- The brief-before-scan race has already occurred: on 08-04 the brief was written at 23:39:05 and the scan ended at 23:48:11. The scan has ended after 23:15 three times (08-04, 08-19, 09-04), not once.
- The 09-23 network outage ran from about 10:05 to 18:10, roughly two hours longer than reported.
- news_watch and chatter have no scheduled job of their own on the host. They ran only inside the disabled morning-briefs job; news_watch state was last written 08-24 and chatter 07-17. The row for morning-briefs does not state this.
- Options leg: three of the five chainless tickers (CLBR, CMMB, GUTS) are trading names with prices through 2026-09-30, so the proposed order's `dead symbols (ADGI, CLBR, CMMB)` is wrong for two of them; CTRA last traded 2026-05-08 per ticker_status.
- For KeepAlive jobs the order's `last 7 exit codes` was not answered as such: qwen-serve's last 7 launchd runs are six bind-failure exits and the current healthy run.
- Log growth not listed by the auditor: ~/.openclaw/fdu/logs/archive.log is 784 MB (the capex, smart-money and collector logs were listed).
- Not covered by either of us: qwen3-coder:30b inference; the five chainless option tickers were not re-derived by calling universe(); per-run exit codes for the brief, efd-probe, collector and dossier do not exist in any log, so their histories rest on artefacts, database rows and envelopes.

### A - verifier's verdict

17 of the 18 RAG calls hold; A8 (smart-money dashboard) should be AMBER, not GREEN. Corrected tally: 5 GREEN, 12 AMBER, 1 RED, 0 UNKNOWN. I re-ran the decisive evidence for every row from the logs, the plists, launchctl print and the databases opened mode=ro.

**The four questions**
1. **HOLX:** cleared, as the auditor says. The last HOLX vendor_error was the 09-21 21:00 run; the nightly leg has exited 0 since the 09-22 21:00 run. The whole job first ended `worst exit 0` on the 09-27 night, and the last four nights are clean.
2. **Options leg:** the last 7 passes are exactly as reported (32 tickers, 27 ok, 0 gaps, 5 no_chain, 0 dropped). The same split holds on all 55 passes since 08-07.
3. **Dev checkout:** no plist names ~/Code/Abelard and every live venv resolves inside abelard-live. The consensus data, both consensus logs and the collector's open database handles are still inside the dev checkout through the consensus/data symlink.
4. **fdu:** healthy, 7 of 7 runs rc=0. It still runs from ~/Code/Abelard-fdu on a branch 20 commits ahead of and 129 behind main, and the exception and its 10-21 expiry are written nowhere in the live tree.

**Where the auditor is wrong or out of date**
- **A8:** the dashboard was restarted by a deploy at 16:31:33 today and now runs c3b7dbb. The buy-cluster CSV export returns HTTP 500 on every request; 21 other routes return 200.
- **A1:** the unfilled price holes were the 09-22 session, not 09-23. The store is also one session behind at every run, which the clean exit code does not show.
- **A2:** each run has 37 [OK] lines, not 38.
- **A3:** CLBR and CMMB are trading names, not dead symbols.
- **A9:** the digest is identical day to day apart from date, counts and ages, so new alerts never appear in it.
- **A18:** the scan has overrun 23:15 three times, and on 08-04 the brief was written before the scan finished. The 09-23 outage ran about 10:05 to 18:10.
- **A11:** I ran one inference against qwen3:8b and it answered in 5.8 s. The row stays AMBER because the service was down 09-04 to 09-19 unnoticed and the runner is unchanged.

**The largest gap**
The audit straddles today's 16:31 deploy and database repair. The scan, brief and dashboard histories describe the previous code; tonight's 22:30 and 23:15 runs are the first on the new code and nobody has verified them.

**Side effects of my verification**
- Probe scripts va1 to va28 and three small output files were written to ~/probes/aud1/ (local copies in the scratchpad under va/). One 3 MB log excerpt was written to /tmp and deleted, which is outside ~/probes/aud1/.
- One probe (va20_pdf.py) hung on a regex and used a CPU core for about two minutes before I killed it.
- About 30 GETs to the smart-money dashboard and 3 to the capex dashboard, each of which the smart-money dashboard logged.
- GETs to Ollama plus one POST /api/generate that loaded qwen3:8b for about 6 s and unloaded it.
- Two GETs to port 3000 that added `Connection refused` lines to qwen-ui.err.log.
- Opening the three WAL databases read-only refreshed their -shm files; no data was written.
- No plist, checkout, launchd state or database content was changed.

Separately, someone else ran a database snapshot (VACUUM INTO _sync_snapshot.db plus rsync) at about 19:09 using the dev checkout's interpreter; that was not me.

## B - The outage root cause

The cause is still not established, so B cannot be closed; what this relay did establish is exactly what failed, for how long, and that the machine is still set up to do it again.

- **What happened:** basilic had no tailnet address from 2026-09-04 01:28:49 to 2026-09-19 22:21:47 EDT (15 d 20 h 53 m). It came back 13 seconds after the owner unlocked the console, with no re-authentication, no process restart and no VPN status change.
- **Ruled out, each with evidence:** node key expiry; App Store build or app not launched; extension needing re-approval; lost login item; unreadable keychain state; a host network problem.
- **What is left:** the Tailscale client was running with its identity loaded and wanting to connect from 01:37:49, yet never completed a control-plane login. The leading suspect is the boot-time login wedging in the auto-locked session that the update's restart creates. Against it, the owner's unlock on 09-04 01:59-02:18 did not release it, and the same Secure Enclave calls succeed today while the screen is locked.
- **Why it cannot be settled from the host:** the Tailscale extension's own log lines last about 2.7 days in the unified log, and its local buffer is root-only and drained on upload. The only surviving copy is at Tailscale. A supervised reboot with logging running would also close it.
- **Corrections to the believed story:** there were two reboots that night (a user restart at 01:29, then the macOS 26.6.2 install at 01:36); the owner was at the console before and after them; and NordVPN auto-updated to 10.6.1, replacing its network extension, in the same minute.
- **What kept working:** every scheduled job ran every night. They are all per-user LaunchAgents, so they ran only because the update logged the user back in automatically. The Ollama and Open WebUI listeners could not bind for the whole window, and Open WebUI is still down.
- **Recurrence (RED):** macOS 26.7.1 and 27.0.1 are pending with automatic install on; Tailscale, NordVPN, FileVault and the no-auto-login setting are unchanged; there is no watchdog and no second remote path. A power cut would stop everything at the disk-unlock screen.

Two things the owner can answer that the logs cannot: what he clicked in the 13 seconds after unlocking on 09-19, and what the system alert at 22:21:55 said (I infer a local-network prompt for Python, as a consequence of the recovery, not its cause).

Nothing was changed on the host. Three things beyond pure reads, all disclosed: `softwareupdate --list` triggers Apple's own background scan; I copied the Tailscale GUI's cache database into `~/probes/aud1/b4_tscache` to read it without touching the original; and probe scripts and outputs are in `~/probes/aud1/` with a `b4_` prefix. I reused one large log dump an earlier relay left there (`b_ul_old_all.txt`) for the 09-05..09-18 lock and display events, and re-queried the 09-04 and 09-19 windows fresh.

Seen in passing, for other sections: about eight hours of failed outbound connections on 2026-09-23 (10:00-18:00) that recovered unattended; the NordVPN app crashed on 09-28; prices finishes "worst exit 1" every night; commits were made in the dev checkout `~/Code/Abelard` on 09-19..09-21.

| ID | RAG | Item | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | **AMBER** | Timeline of the outage, with timestamps and sources | Verified from the host: basilic had no tailnet address from the 2026-09-04 01:28:49 shutdown until 2026-09-19 22:21:47 (15 d 20 h 53 m), ending 13 seconds after the owner unlocked the console. The believed story holds with three corrections: there were two reboots (01:29 user restart, 01:36 macOS 26.6.2), the owner was at the console before and after them, and NordVPN auto-updated to 10.6.1 in the same minute. |
| B2 | **AMBER** | What kept working during 09-04..09-21 | Every scheduled job ran every night of the outage and host internet was normal; what was lost was remote access and the three tailnet-bound services. The jobs are all per-user LaunchAgents, so they ran only because the update's restart logged the user back in automatically; prices already finished "worst exit 1" before, during and after, and the Open WebUI lane has been down since 09-04 and still is. |
| B3 | **UNKNOWN** | THE CAUSE - why Tailscale stayed disconnected for two weeks and then came back | Not established. Candidates 1-4 are each ruled out by evidence: the Tailscale client was running with its identity loaded and WantRunning=true from 01:37:49, yet never completed a control-plane login until a human used the console on 09-19. Best-supported hypothesis: the boot-time login wedged in the auto-locked post-update session, but the same unlock on 09-04 01:59 did not release it, so the mechanism is unproven and the item stays open. |
| B4 | **AMBER** | What would prevent a recurrence (proposal only) | Nothing is in place today: no watchdog, no second remote path, no control over when the machine reboots. The prevention that does not depend on knowing the exact cause is to stop relying on the GUI/login-session Tailscale build on a headless host and to stop unattended reboots. |
| B5 | **RED** | Could it recur TODAY - is the machine in the state that caused it | Yes. Every ingredient of 09-04 is unchanged and a restart-requiring macOS update is already pending with automatic install on, so the next console unlock can arm another overnight reboot. A power cut would be worse: with FileVault on and no auto-login the machine stops at the disk-unlock screen and nothing runs at all. |

### B - evidence

**B5 - RED - Could it recur TODAY - is the machine in the state that caused it**

- Evidence: `softwareupdate --list`: "* Label: macOS Tahoe 26.7.1-25G241 ... Action: restart" and "* Label: macOS 27.0.1-26A434 ... Action: restart"; `defaults read .../com.apple.SoftwareUpdate`: "AutomaticallyInstallMacOSUpdates = 1", "LastLoginWindowHarvestMethod = ReqAuto". The 09-04 chain started the same way: "01:27:34 loginwindow: grabbed user ID 501" then "SUOSUScheduler: ARMED". Same software: `tailscale version` "1.98.8", bundle "io.tailscale.ipn.macsys"; NordVPN "com.nordvpn.macos.Shield (10.6.1/366) [activated enabled]". `fdesetup status`: "FileVault is On."; `defaults read /Library/Preferences/com.apple.loginwindow autoLoginUser`: "does not exist"; `pmset -g`: "autorestart 1". All 15 jobs are ~/Library/LaunchAgents plists (need a logged-in user). Single network path: `ifconfig en0` "status: inactive"; en1 (Wi-Fi) "inet 192.168.0.12"; Wi-Fi dropped twice last night (ext log "01:13:09.852 swiftNetMon: ... unsatisfied (No network route), interface: en1[802.11]" and again 01:59:50), each recovering in about 90 s. Current client state is not clean: `tailscale status` prints "# Health check: - You are logged out. The last login error was: register request: Post \"https://controlplane.tailscale.com/machine/register\": connection attempts aborted by context: context deadline exceeded" while "BackendState = Running", "Peer Orban Online= True Active= True". That is the unfinished interactive login long-polling, not a live outage.
- Verifier agrees: RED holds as a risk verdict: the cause is unknown, so nothing shows the machine is out of the failing state, and every exposure I re-checked is unchanged. Re-run: RecommendedUpdates lists macOS Tahoe 26.7.1 and macOS 27.0.1 (read from the SoftwareUpdate prefs; I did not run `softwareupdate --list`). AutomaticallyInstallMacOSUpdates = 1, LastLoginWindowHarvestMethod = ReqAuto. Tailscale 1.98.8 macsys. NordVPN Shield 10.6.1 [activated enabled]. FileVault On, autoLoginUser absent, autorestart 1. en0 inactive, en1 192.168.0.12 the only path. All 15 jobs are per-user LaunchAgents. Caveats on the verdict text: - "Every ingredient of 09-04 is unchanged" is asserted without knowing which ingredient mattered, and one differs today: the NordVPN app and its flow-divert proxy have not run since the 09-28 crash. startOnBoot = 1, so the next login restores the 09-04 arrangement. - The 09-04 update was not an unattended overnight install. The 01:27:34 unlock did arm a 02:00 install, but the owner restarted at 01:28:33 (which disarmed it) and ran the update by hand from Settings at about 01:31. The unattended path exists but was not what happened. - The health line has changed since the auditor read it: now "register request: http 502: backend not found or not available" with a request id from 20:07 EDT today. It is a retry loop that errors, not a quiet long-poll; BackendState is still Running with Orban online. - A new exposure: host DNS runs through the Tailscale forwarder, so an extension fault now also breaks name resolution for the jobs. About 4,400 connections a day to 1.1.1.1:443 and 1.0.0.1:853 are being routed into the tunnel and timing out ("open-conn-track: timeout opening").
- Proposed order: Until SM-B-HARDEN lands: do not unlock the basilic console or approve any update without a plan to verify Tailscale afterwards from a second device, and treat any reboot as requiring someone physically present.

**B3 - UNKNOWN - THE CAUSE - why Tailscale stayed disconnected for two weeks and then came back**

- Evidence: 1. Key expiry - ruled out. `tailscale status --json` on basilic: "Self.Created = 2026-07-14T04:43:10.629248295Z", "Self.KeyExpiry = 2027-01-10T04:43:10Z" (exactly Created + 180 d, so never re-issued); same values seen from orban. Ext log today: "control: RegisterReq: onode=[p/wlu] node=[8fxtv] fup=true" - the old node key is still the live one. 2. App Store build / app not launched - ruled out. `defaults read .../Info CFBundleIdentifier`: "io.tailscale.ipn.macsys" (Standalone); GUI pid 1146 started "Sep 4 01:37:41", extension pid 1213 "01:37:43". 3. Extension re-approval - ruled out. `systemextensionsctl list`: "io.tailscale.ipn.macsys.network-extension (1.98.8/101.98.8) [activated enabled]"; extension dir birth 2026-07-14; NE session "Connected", "DisconnectedCount : 0", "LastStatusChangeTime : 09/04/2026 01:37:49". 4. Login item lost - ruled out. Prefs "TailscaleStartOnLogin = True"; GUI launched 31 s after console login. Keychain state unreadable - ruled out. Tailscale GUI NSURLCache (copied to ~/probes/aud1/b4_tscache): "2026-09-04 05:37:49 UTC /localapi/v0/prefs \| 'WantRunning': True, 'LoggedOut': False" and "/profiles/ \| 'n_profiles': 1, 'has_NodeID': [True]". Host network - ruled out (B2). Unattended recovery works in steady state: `tailscale debug metrics` "controlclient_map_response_keepalive 18298" at 19:16:28, rising 266 in 14,670 s; that total equals about 11.7 days of connection, i.e. none before 09-19. Reconnects on 10-01 01:14 and 02:01 succeeded with the keybag locked (no "AKS: Unlocked" since 09-19 22:21:34). For the hypothesis: keybag "AKS: Locking" at 01:37:42.281, 1.2 s before the extension started, "Locked" 01:37:51.904; today the extension logs "(CryptoTokenKit) SEPClientObject: operation" and "LAContext ... for transfer to pid:1066" (ctkd) at every register/map request, e.g. 01:14:09.585 and 01:14:33.938. Against it: console unlocked 09-04 01:59:19-02:18:31 with socat bind failures continuing every ~128 s; and those Secure Enclave calls succeed now while locked. Other variable in that boot: NordVPN 10.6.1 replaced its Shield network extension (`stat` birth "2026-09-04 01:28:39 /Library/SystemExtensions/05EE85D1-..."). No trace of interference survives; Tailscale ran alongside it 09-19..09-28. The alert clicked at 22:22:35 ("current generation (46) does not equal posted generation (47)") came 48 s after the tailnet address returned, so it is a consequence; the only user decision in the NE plist is "'org.python.python' DenyMulticast=False PrefSet=True". Missing evidence: (a) the extension's own log from 09-04 01:37:43 and 09-19 22:21:34-47. Unified-log retention for that process is about 2.7 days (oldest line now "2026-09-29 04:24:51"); the local buffer "/Library/Tailscale/ipn.log..log1.txt" is root-only and 0 bytes because it uploads to Tailscale on reconnect, so the only copy is at Tailscale. (b) What the owner clicked in the 13 s after unlocking on 09-19; an interactive login was started and never finished ("AuthURL = <present>"). (c) The far-end last-seen time (orban's C:\ProgramData\Tailscale\Logs: "Access is denied").
- **Verifier changed AMBER to UNKNOWN**: The cause is not established and the row should be UNKNOWN, not AMBER. AMBER needs a best-supported hypothesis; the one offered is refuted. Candidates 1-4 - I agree they are ruled out. Re-run: Self.KeyExpiry 2027-01-10T04:43:10Z = Created + 180 d, and Self.PublicKey short form is [p/wlu], the same key the log shows as onode. Bundle id io.tailscale.ipn.macsys 1.98.8. `systemextensionsctl list`: Tailscale extension [activated enabled], directory born 07-14. NE session Connected since 09/04 01:37:49, DisconnectedCount 0. TailscaleStartOnLogin = 1 and login-item-helper enabled. GUI cache /prefs at 05:37:49Z: WantRunning true, LoggedOut false, one profile. The "wedged in the auto-locked post-update session" hypothesis fails four ways: - The auditor's own two: the 01:59 unlock did not release it, and the Secure Enclave calls succeed while locked. - The address was already absent at 01:31:55 in the first boot, with the owner logged in, before 26.6.2 was installed. - The backend recovered by 22:20:35 on 09-19, before the display woke and 59 s before the unlock (see B1). New constraints from this pass: - The control client was never recreated: authRoutine/mapRoutine/updateRoutine are goroutines 200-202, created at boot. Recovery was not a backend restart. - The extension was not hung: about 2.77 million goroutines were spawned between boot and recovery (about 121 per minute). - No NE configuration change coincided with recovery. Generations 45, 46 and 47 landed at 09-04 01:38:45, 09-10 19:36:01 and 09-19 22:22:35. Candidate 5 was not properly tested. NordVPN is not only an endpoint-security extension: the NE plist holds "NordVPN Threat Protection Pro" with TunnelType 2 (transparent proxy), Enabled = True, provider com.nordvpn.macos.Shield. The NordVPN app (pid 1141) started in the same second as Tailscale and ran until its 09-28 20:59:16 crash. Connection paths carry "flow divert agg: 2" from 09-04 01:37:51 through 09-28. NordVPN 10.6.1 was the only software change before the first failing boot. This is a lead, not a cause: the same proxy was diverting flows 09-19..09-28 while Tailscale worked, and nothing explains a release at 22:20. "Host network ruled out (B2)" shows the host could reach the internet. It does not show the extension's own sockets could. The log that would settle it is the extension's own, 09-04 01:29 to 09-19 22:21:47. It is gone from the host: the earliest extension line surviving is 2026-09-28 22:11:29 (in the earlier relay's dump; now 09-29), and /Library/Tailscale/ipn.log..log1.txt is root-only and 695 bytes. Surviving copies, all off-host: Tailscale's log service, the tailnet admin console (audit log and machine page for 09-20 02:17-02:22 UTC), and orban's Tailscale logs (admin-only). The unfinished interactive login is real ("doLogin(regen=false, hasUrl=true)", "RegisterReq: onode=[p/wlu] node=[8fxtv] fup=true") but its start is not established; the earliest surviving line is 09-29 15:26.
- Proposed order: Order SM-B-REPRO: reproduce under observation. With the owner at the console, start `log stream --info --predicate 'process == "io.tailscale.ipn.macsys.network-extension"'` to a file, reboot once by normal login and once via Software Update's restart (auto-login then auto-lock), and record whether the tailnet IP appears before any unlock. In parallel run `tailscale bugreport` and ask Tailscale support for node Basilic's client log for 2026-09-04 05:37Z to 2026-09-20 02:22Z. Ask the owner what he clicked at 22:21 on 09-19.

**B1 - AMBER - Timeline of the outage, with timestamps and sources**

- Evidence: All times EDT. `last reboot`: "reboot time Fri Sep 4 01:36 / shutdown time Fri Sep 4 01:33 / reboot time Fri Sep 4 01:29 / shutdown time Fri Sep 4 01:28". `sysctl -n kern.boottime`: "Fri Sep 4 01:36:17 2026". `uptime`: "up 27 days, 17:29". `softwareupdate --history`: "macOS Tahoe 26.6.2 26.6.2 09/04/2026, 01:33:07". /var/log/install.log: "01:27:34 loginwindow[491]: SUOSULoginCredentialPolicy: grabbed user ID 501" (owner unlocked the console); "01:28:31 installer[42629]: Product archive .../org.sparkle-project.Sparkle/.../NordVPN_Sideload.pkg"; "01:28:33 softwareupdated: Got com.apple.system.loginwindow.logoutNoReturn"; "01:28:38 installd: Installed \"\" (10.6.1)"; "01:31:49 SoftwareUpdateSettingsExtension[1643]: SecKeychainStash() succeeded". `last`: "wafflehaus console Fri Sep 4 01:29 - 01:32", "wafflehaus console Fri Sep 4 01:37 still logged in". `ps -o lstart`: Tailscale GUI pid 1146 "Fri Sep 4 01:37:41", extension pid 1213 "01:37:43" (same PIDs today). `scutil --nc status 68226498-...`: "Connected ... ConnectCount : 1 ... DisconnectedCount : 0 ... LastStatusChangeTime : 09/04/2026 01:37:49". `log show --info` 09-04: "01:37:42.281 AKS: Locking", "01:37:51.904 AKS: Locked", "01:59:19.269 applekeystored MKBUnlockDevice result: (0), caller pid: 602", "02:18:31.876 AKS: Locked". ~/abelard-qwen/logs/qwen-ui.err.log: "2026/09/04 01:28:49 socat[65467] W exiting on signal 15"; "2026/09/04 01:39:59 socat[1299] E bind(5, {LEN=16 AF=2 100.106.84.115:3000}, 16): Can't assign requested address" repeating every ~128 s (10,577 lines, 27-28 per hour including 02:00-02:59 on 09-04) to "2026/09/19 22:19:43 socat[69206] E bind(...)". Recovery: /var/log/powermanagement/2026.09.19.asl "Sep 19 22:20:57 powerd: Display is turned on"; log show "22:21:34.330 applekeystored MKBUnlockDevice result: (0)"; `stat` "2026-09-19 22:21:47 \| /Library/Preferences/com.apple.networkextension.cache.plist"; Tailscale GUI cache "2026-09-20 02:21:47 UTC /localapi/v0/suggest-exit-node"; qwen-serve.err.log "time=2026-09-19T22:21:53.412-04:00 msg=\"Listening on 100.106.84.115:11434\""; "22:38:38.422 AKS: Locked". First remote work after: `git reflog` ~/Code/Abelard "HEAD@{2026-09-19 23:42:07 -0400}: commit". No sleep: `pmset -g` "sleep 0"; ASL 09-17..09-21 has no Sleep/Wake/Shutdown lines. Log reach: `pmset -g log` starts "2026-09-24 19:08:57"; /var/log/powermanagement starts 2026.09.17.asl; install.log starts 2026-04-25; unified log archive starts "Tue Sep 1" but Tailscale-extension lines only from "2026-09-29 04:24:51". The 09-05..09-18 display and lock events come from the dump an earlier relay took at 15:42 today (~/probes/aud1/b_ul_old_all.txt); I re-queried the 09-04 and 09-19 windows fresh.
- **Verifier changed GREEN to AMBER**: Every timestamp I re-ran holds, but the timeline misplaces both ends of the outage, so it is not GREEN. Re-run and confirmed: `last reboot` (shutdown 01:28 / reboot 01:29 / shutdown 01:33 / reboot 01:36), kern.boottime 01:36:17, `softwareupdate --history` 26.6.2 at 01:33:07, install.log 01:27:34 credential harvest and 01:28:31-38 NordVPN pkg, `last` console logins 01:29-01:32 and 01:37 still logged in, Tailscale pids 1146/1213 started 01:37:41/43, `scutil --nc status` Connected / ConnectCount 1 / LastStatusChangeTime 09/04 01:37:49, 10,577 socat bind failures ending 22:19:43, ollama "Listening on 100.106.84.115:11434" at 22:21:53.412, networkextension.cache.plist mtime 22:21:47, display wake 22:20:57, MKBUnlockDevice 22:21:34.330 by pid 602 (loginwindow), 09-04 unlock 01:59:19 after display wake 01:58:26. Correction 1 - the fault predates the macOS update. qwen-ui.err.log line 5: "2026/09/04 01:31:55 socat[866] E bind(... 100.106.84.115:3000): Can't assign requested address". That is the FIRST boot (colima started 01:29:43), 2 min after login, with the owner logged in and using the console (SoftwareUpdateSettingsExtension pid 1643 opened 01:31:21), and before 26.6.2 was installed at 01:33:07. The GUI cache agrees: /prefs at 05:29:44Z shows WantRunning false and that boot never fetched suggest-exit-node, where the three July boots did so within 1-5 s. The auditor quoted 01:39:59 as the first bind failure. Correction 2 - recovery began before anyone touched the console. `tailscale debug daemon-goroutines` at 21:18:34 shows the two peerAPI listeners (goroutines 2774708/9, initPeerAPIListen) blocked 17218 minutes, i.e. created no later than 22:20:35 on 09-19. That is before the USB/display wake (22:20:55-57) and 59 s before the unlock. Clock drift cannot explain it: CLOCK_MONOTONIC minus CLOCK_MONOTONIC_RAW is 1.34 s over the whole uptime. The GUI's bus-watch connection is 7138 minutes old, exactly 7 days later, so the GUI also restarted its watch in that same minute. The address did appear at about 22:21:47, but "ending 13 seconds after the owner unlocked" implies a trigger the evidence contradicts. This rests on Go's wait-time semantics (wait measured from the first GC after blocking), which I am fairly but not fully sure of. Smaller points: NordVPN 10.6.0 to 10.6.1 was installed by Sparkle during the 01:28 logout, so it was caused by the restart, not coincident with it. The macOS update was started by hand from System Settings at about 01:31. The unified log holds nothing at all for the first boot (query 01:26-01:36:10 returned zero lines).

[OK] options_chains date=2026-09-11 ... ok=27 gaps=0 ... <<< exit 0 2026-09-12T03:10:54Z". prices.log: "=== prices night done, worst exit 1 2026-09-NNT01:2x" for every night 09-04..09-22. `ls ~/.openclaw/smart_money/scans \| grep BRIEF_202609`: SMART_MONEY_BRIEF_20260903.pdf through _20260922.pdf, no gaps. Briefs SMART_MONEY_BRIEF_20260901..0930 have no gaps. 15 LaunchAgents; /Library/LaunchDaemons holds only com.nordvpn.macos.helper.plist. qwen-serve bind failures 135,393; socat 10,577. Collector error lines were 1-56 per day through the outage. Corrections: - prices is no longer "worst exit 1" every night. It was exit 1 through the 09-27T01:26Z run and has been "worst exit 0" for the four runs 09-28..10-01. The proposed order should say so. - The 09-23 10:00-18:00 event was name resolution, not generic connection failure: 6,588 of 6,621 lines are "NameResolutionError ... nodename nor servname provided". **B4 - AMBER - What would prevent a recurrence (proposal only)**

- Evidence: `ls /Library/LaunchDaemons`: only "com.nordvpn.macos.helper.plist"; "ls: /Library/LaunchDaemons/com.tailscale.tailscaled.plist: No such file or directory" (no daemon-mode Tailscale). `defaults read /Library/Preferences/com.apple.SoftwareUpdate`: "AutomaticallyInstallMacOSUpdates = 1". `netstat`: sshd "*.22 LISTEN" and screen sharing "*.5900 LISTEN" exist but are reachable only over the LAN or Tailscale. NordVPN prefs: "SUAutomaticallyUpdate = True"; its app crashed 09-28 ("NordVPN-2026-09-28-205938.ips ... EXC_CRASH SIGABRT"). LaunchAgents kept running throughout the outage (B2), so a local check of the tailnet address would have fired on 09-04; a plain liveness ping would have stayed green.
- Verifier agrees: AMBER holds; the evidence re-runs clean and the proposal is sound whatever the cause turns out to be. Re-run: /Library/LaunchDaemons contains only com.nordvpn.macos.helper.plist (no tailscaled daemon). `defaults read /Library/Preferences/com.apple.SoftwareUpdate`: AutomaticallyInstallMacOSUpdates = 1. sshd *.22 and screen sharing *.5900 listening. NordVPN SUAutomaticallyUpdate = 1 and startOnBoot = 1. NordVPN-2026-09-28-205938.ips exists. Changes I would make to the proposed orders: - SM-B-REPRO is designed around the unlock ("record whether the tailnet IP appears before any unlock"). The fault appeared on a plain restart with the user logged in, so the reproduction should vary NordVPN instead: reboot with the NordVPN app and its Threat Protection proxy enabled, then with them removed, logging the extension each time. - "Ask the owner what he clicked at 22:21" is the wrong question. Ask what happened between about 22:17 and 22:20:35 on 09-19, before he sat down: router or modem touched, another device joining the LAN, anything done in the Tailscale admin console from a phone. - Add: pull the tailnet audit log and machine page for that window. - Item 5 should name the Threat Protection Pro transparent proxy explicitly, not just the app and auto-update. - Add: stop the host's DNS depending on Tailscale (accept-dns off on basilic), given the 09-23 eight-hour resolution failure. - Add: remove ~/probes/aud1/b4_tscache/Cache.db after the audit. Five cached responses in it carry a PrivateNodeKey value; the file is 0644 (home is 0750, single user, so exposure is low).
- Proposed order: Order SM-B-HARDEN (sequence after SM-B-REPRO, or in parallel if the owner prefers): 1. Replace the Standalone GUI Tailscale with the open-source `tailscaled` run as a system LaunchDaemon (no user session, Keychain or Secure Enclave dependency); verify it comes up after a reboot with nobody logged in. 2. Set automatic macOS update install off on basilic; reboots become attended, done with someone able to reach the console. 3. Build INFRA-B1 as an assertion bundle that includes "utun has 100.106.84.115" and "control keepalives advancing", alerting off-host over a non-Tailscale channel. 4. Add a second remote path that does not ride on Tailscale. 5. Remove NordVPN (app, helper, Shield extension) from basilic, or at least turn off its auto-update. 6. Clear the dangling interactive login (cancel or complete it deliberately). 7. Decide the FileVault-versus-power-loss trade-off explicitly (see B5).

### B - found by the verifier, missed by the auditor

- The fault was already present in the FIRST boot on 09-04 (socat bind failure 01:31:55; GUI cache WantRunning false at 05:29:44Z, never reached Running), with the owner logged in and before macOS 26.6.2 was installed at 01:33:07. The section treats the OS update and its auto-locked restart as the starting point.
- Backend recovery predates the console unlock: peerAPI listener goroutines are 17218 minutes old at 21:18:34 on 10-01 (created by 22:20:35 on 09-19), and measured clock drift is 1.34 s over the whole uptime. The section presents the unlock as the trigger and builds its hypothesis, its reproduction order and its question to the owner on that.
- NordVPN has an enabled transparent-proxy NE configuration ("NordVPN Threat Protection Pro", TunnelType 2, provider com.nordvpn.macos.Shield). Its app (pid 1141) launched in the same second as Tailscale and ran until 09-28 20:59:16; flows carried "flow divert agg: 2" from 09-04 01:37:51 through 09-28. The section describes Shield only as an extension that was replaced and says no trace of interference survives.
- NordVPN 10.6.0 to 10.6.1 was the only software change before the first failing boot, and it was installed by Sparkle during the 01:28 logout (caused by the restart, not coincident with it).
- The control client was never recreated (goroutines 200-202 date from boot) and the extension spawned about 2.77 million goroutines before recovery, so it was neither restarted nor hung. Neither fact was collected.
- Owner-accessible evidence not named: the tailnet admin console audit log and machine page for 2026-09-20 02:17-02:22 UTC. Only Tailscale support and orban's local logs were named.
- Client metrics were used only for the keepalive count. Unexamined: derp_home_no_change_no_control 3,694; netcheck_https_measure 102,508 (about 3,660 UDP-blocked netchecks); 323,343 IPv4 STUN sent against 14,025 received. I could not attribute these in time; the 09-23 eight-hour event is the most likely source, but that is an inference.
- The 09-23 event is a DNS failure (6,588 NameResolutionError lines), and since recovery the host's default resolver is Tailscale's forwarder on utun4. That coupling belongs in B4/B5.
- The copied GUI cache at ~/probes/aud1/b4_tscache/Cache.db contains PrivateNodeKey values in five cached responses, mode 0644. The auditor disclosed the copy but not that it holds key material.
- prices.log has reported "worst exit 0" since the 09-28T01:26Z run; the summary and the proposed order still say it exits 1 every night.
- The unified log contains nothing for the first 09-04 boot (01:26-01:36:10 returns zero lines), which should be stated alongside the other log-reach limits.
- The start time of the unfinished interactive login (new node key [8fxtv]) is not established; the earliest surviving line is 09-29 15:26, so tying it to the 13 seconds after the 09-19 unlock is a guess.

### B - verifier's verdict

Section B cannot be closed: the cause is still unknown, and B3 should read UNKNOWN rather than AMBER. Candidates 1-4 are ruled out and I confirmed each on re-run, but the one hypothesis offered (login wedged in the auto-locked post-update session, released by the unlock) is refuted, so there is no best-supported hypothesis left to justify AMBER.

- **B1 (GREEN to AMBER):** every timestamp re-ran correctly, but the timeline misplaces both ends. The tailnet address was already absent at 01:31:55 in the first boot, before macOS 26.6.2 was installed and with the owner logged in. The Tailscale backend had recovered by 22:20:35 on 09-19, before the display woke and 59 seconds before the unlock.
- **B2 (AMBER, agree):** jobs ran every night. Two corrections: prices has exited 0 for the last four nights, and the 09-23 event was eight hours of DNS failure.
- **B3 (AMBER to UNKNOWN):** the deciding log is the Tailscale extension's own for 09-04 01:29 to 09-19 22:21:47, and it no longer exists on the host. Copies may exist at Tailscale, in the tailnet admin audit log, and on orban.
- **B4 (AMBER, agree):** the hardening order stands. The reproduction order should vary NordVPN rather than the unlock.
- **B5 (RED, agree):** nothing shows the machine is out of the failing state. The NordVPN app is not running today but relaunches at the next login.

The strongest untested lead is NordVPN: version 10.6.1 was the only software change before the first failing boot, and its Threat Protection transparent proxy was diverting flows throughout the outage. It is not a cause yet, because the same proxy ran for nine days after recovery while Tailscale worked.

The pre-unlock recovery finding rests on how Go reports goroutine wait times; I am fairly but not fully sure of that reading.

Nothing was changed on the host. I wrote probe scripts and their outputs under /Users/wafflehaus/probes/aud1/ with a bv_ prefix, and ran read-only commands including `tailscale debug metrics` and `tailscale debug daemon-goroutines`. I did not run `softwareupdate --list`.

## C - Freshness per table

Section C, measured read-only on basilic between 14:47 and 19:09 EDT on 2026-10-01 (before tonight's 21:00 price job and 22:30 scan; nothing in these tables changes in that window). 15 rows: 9 GREEN, 6 AMBER, 0 RED, 0 UNKNOWN. Nothing is broken; every AMBER is a lag or a blind spot with a named cause.

What "expected" meant: for each table I used the source's own state where I could read it without side effects - EDGAR's daily form index and submissions JSON (GET only, using the daemon's configured EDGAR_CONTACT, never printed), the House Clerk index the scan itself downloaded at 22:30 last night, and the host's own eFD probe log - and otherwise the last completed session or run as of the end of last night's cycle (session 2026-09-30).

The headline answers:
- Form 4 universal is through 2026-09-29, not yesterday. That is by design (the scan walks indexes ending the day before it runs); EDGAR's 09-30 index holds 293 Form 4s that land tonight. The leg never ingests Form 4/A.
- The dark-date hole does not exist in this table. All nine business days 09-08..09-18 are present, were ingested live each following night, and four sampled days match EDGAR's count exactly with zero missing accessions. The only weekday gaps in the table's whole history are 14 federal closures.
- House and Senate PTR are both current against their sources (403 of 403 Clerk PTR DocIDs accounted for; Senate 30-day count 10 = eFD's 10). The Senate delta leg is ingesting, last on 09-28.
- 13F: Q2 is newest and complete for originals (25 of 28; EDGAR confirms the other three have no Q2 13F-HR). Zero Q3 arrivals, and zero exist on EDGAR yet.
- Options: 27 names, zero gaps, 55 consecutive nights. Registry: 43 entries. Future-dated spans: zero. universe-sync ran last night, exit 0.

Findings that should become orders:
1. Congress annuals (C6): the House index refresh uses max_age_days=1 on a job that hits it at the same minute nightly, so last night it read the 09-29 cache, reported OK new=0, and missed a filing published 09-29 (DocID 10081183). Same class as the frozen-cache bug fixed on 09-21, intermittent rather than permanent.
2. 13F amendments (C7): the leg matches '13F-HR' exactly. First Eagle's Q2 amendment (filed 2026-08-24) and Q1 amendment are not held; Pershing's Q2 13F-NT is invisible; two Q1 filings are seen with zero holdings rows, cause not established.
3. Price substrate (C11, C13): prices_raw is one session behind (09-29) by construction, and reference_series stores a NULL vendor_null row for SPY/IVV/RSP/XLE on 09-30, so MAX(date) reads current when the value is not. Reconcile rows for 09-22 and 09-23 exist only as failures.
4. Options (C9): no holiday calendar - Labor Day is stored as a session and the next day's oi_asof points at it; 5 scoped names have never served a chain.
5. Smaller: 4/A not ingested in the universal leg (C1); one House PTR fetch_failed since 07-17 never retried (C3); 478 price_spans overstate coverage (C14); the repo fallback registry has 21 entries (C15); the eFD finding expires 2026-11-06 (C5).

Not verified: Senate annual-report completeness against eFD itself (C6) - there is no cached index, and opening an eFD session means accepting its terms, which I did not do.

One thing to know about the audit itself: empty sidecar files prices.db-wal (0 bytes) and prices.db-shm (32 KB) appeared in ~/.openclaw/prices at 15:42:00 today. They were absent at 14:38 and their birth time coincides with another section's probe (a13_pricesdb.py, uploaded 15:41:57) opening the database mode=ro; I did not confirm the author beyond that timing. My own reads of prices.db used immutable=1 and created nothing. The files are what SQLite makes when a WAL database is opened read-only, and are harmless, but they are a change on the host.

Probe scripts are in ~/probes/aud1/ on basilic, all prefixed c_ (c_sm1.py .. c_sm7.py, c_px0.py .. c_px2.py, c_edgar.py, c_edgar13f.py, c_13f2.py, c_opt.py, c_ann.py, c_reg.py, plus the c_*.sh readers). Nothing else was written, no database was opened for write, no git or launchctl state was touched.

EXPECTED per the order = 2026-09-30 (yesterday); expected per code = 2026-09-29, form4_universal.py ingest_recent: `Walks the last lookback_days of daily indexes ending YESTERDAY (never today...)`. GAP = 1 filing day: EDGAR form.20260930.idx fetched read-only -> `2026-09-30 index HTTP 200 EDGAR distinct Form 4 = 293 (4/A = 7) \| db row None \| NOT in form4_backfill_seen = 293`. 4/A: form4.py `if not line.startswith("4 "): continue`; on 6 sampled days `4/A accessions seen = 0 of 16/13/5/18/13/7`. History: `rows with parse_fail>0: (26 days, 65 filings)`, none in the last 28 rows.

My own EDGAR GET of form.20260930.idx: HTTP 200, 293 distinct Form 4 + 7 4/A, 293 not in form4_backfill_seen, no universal_days row. form4_universal.py:164-171 confirms the anchor is today-1 with lookback_days=7. 4/A: 0 of 4/A accessions seen on all 9 days I sampled (17/7/3/7/13/12/6/14/7); form4.py:51 `startswith("4 ")`. AMBER holds: one filing day behind the order's 'yesterday', by design. Two additions: (a) the 7-day lookback means an outage longer than 7 days would NOT self-heal; (b) each day 0-5 indexed Form 4s are marked seen but have no row in form4_transactions or form4_derivatives while parse_fail=0 (e.g. 5 on 09-04, 3 on 09-15) - not established whether those are holdings-only filings or silent zero-row parses.
- Proposed order: Rule on whether the universal leg should ingest Form 4/A (0 of 72 sampled amendments are held), and whether a D+0 late pass is wanted; if D-1 lag is accepted, write it into the freshness expectation so 'through yesterday' stops being the test.

- C11: the reconcile backlog is 13 as_of dates that never passed plus one (2026-09-09) with no row, not the two the row names; and 8 quarantined August rows have no fill.
- The summary's statement that 'nothing in these tables changes in that window' is false. abelard-live was fast-forwarded d135a1d -> c3b7dbb at 16:31:33 EDT and a date_sanity repair wrote to smart_money_v0.db at 16:32 (backup pre_date_sanity_20261001-163159.db; manifests: 799 tz-normalised tx_dates, 45 quarantined, 371 non-trading marks, 362 OGE date conversions; new date_flag / date_subclass / tx_date_suggested columns on form4_transactions and congress_trades). Rows measured before 16:32 (C1 at 14:47) describe the pre-repair database. I re-measured everything after the repair and no freshness number moved, but the audit should say the database changed under it.
- The order asked for a per-day filed_date count for 2026-09-01..2026-09-30; the row's evidence lists only 09-08..09-18. The full month is clean (every weekday present except Labor Day 09-07 and the pending 09-30), but that was asserted rather than shown.
- Universal Form 4 ingest has lookback_days=7 (form4_universal.py:160): an outage longer than a week leaves a permanent hole with no self-heal. Directly relevant to the dark-date question and not mentioned.
- Senate annual-report completeness against eFD is still unverified by either of us (no cached index; a session requires accepting eFD's terms). It should be carried as an explicit open item rather than folded into C6's AMBER.
- eFD's 30-day PTR count rose from 10 to 11 at 18:13 today: one new Senate PTR exists that the database does not yet hold. Expected to land at 22:30 tonight; nobody has confirmed it will.

### C - verifier's verdict

14 of 15 RAGs hold; one GREEN should be AMBER. Corrected tally: 8 GREEN, 7 AMBER, 0 RED, 0 UNKNOWN. I re-ran the decisive query or fetch for every row on basilic between 19:13 and about 19:30 EDT, read-only, and the auditor's numbers reproduced exactly in every table.

The one disagreement is C10 (Smart Money prices through last session). The table's max date and SPY are current, and I confirmed SPY's 09-30 close of 762.63 against a live Yahoo fetch. But 69 of 3152 requested names are not through 09-30, and 35 of the 37 failures are the same names every night. BRK.B is one of them: the scan requests it with a dot, Yahoo rejects it, and the stored BRK-B series stopped on 2026-07-23 while 22 congressional BRK.B trades were disclosed this year. The leg reports this as OK. That is a standing caveat, so AMBER.

The GREENs I was asked to distrust all stand on more than a log line:
- **C2 dark dates:** all nine dark business days are now matched against EDGAR's daily index with zero missing accessions (the auditor's four plus my five).
- **C3 House PTR:** checked against the Clerk's live index, not the cached copy: 403 of 403 PTR DocIDs accounted for.
- **C5 Senate delta:** verified from per-night scan JSON item counts and matching ingest timestamps.
- **C8 Q3 13F:** zero on EDGAR for the registry's own 28 CIKs.
- **C12, C14, C15:** verified in the database and files directly.

Three AMBER rows are right but understated:
- **C6:** three House annual amendments are outstanding, not one; the prior-cycle 2025 index was never diffed.
- **C11:** thirteen sessions have never passed reconcile and 09-09 has no reconcile row, not just 09-22 and 09-23.
- **C7:** five 13F accessions are seen with no holdings rows, not two.

One statement in the auditor's summary is wrong: the database did change during the audit window. The live tree was fast-forwarded at 16:31 and a date-sanity repair wrote to smart_money_v0.db at 16:32. No freshness figure moved, but the report should say so.

Still open: Senate annual-report completeness against eFD (neither of us verified it), and one new Senate PTR that appeared on eFD at 18:13 today and should land tonight.

My probes are in /Users/wafflehaus/probes/aud1/ on basilic, prefixed vc_ (vc_sm1.py to vc_sm5.py, vc_px1.py to vc_px3.py, vc_edgar.py, vc_13f.py, vc_reg_house.py, vc_logs1.sh, vc_logs2.sh, vc_misc.sh, and vc_13f_ciks.json, a CIK list one probe wrote). Nothing else was written; no database was opened for write; prices.db was opened immutable; no git or launchctl state was touched.

## D - Every view

Section D: 46 rows - 32 GREEN, 7 AMBER, 7 RED. All 15 routes in dashboard.ROUTES answer HTTP 200 with populated tables or explicit empty-state sentences, served by the process restarted at 16:31:33 EDT from abelard-live at c3b7dbb; the live tree was clean after probing.

The J6 regression itself has not recurred: all 14 printable views return a PDF with their own title and table and no front-brief content, and requests with nothing to print answer 400. The front-brief footer is sane and reconciles exactly with the database.

The seven REDs come from three defects:
- **Buy-cluster CSV is down (D34).** Every /clusters.csv?which=buy request returns HTTP 500 while the screen shows 354 clusters. Rows gained an `unmapped` key in 883baf7 (2026-09-22) that the export's column contract does not list; the existing test only checks the header on its fixture.
- **Committee drill-down loses its committee (D25, D44; AMBER D10).** `_qs()` has no `cmte` key, so on /committees?cmte=X the print button prints the all-committees roll, both CSV links export the roll, and sort/pager links bounce back to the roll. The handlers work when `cmte` is typed by hand.
- **Four PDFs run off the page (D24 breadth_yoy, D26 trades, D27 flows, D29 sentinels).** Any view with 18 or more columns is laid out wider than the paper and centred, so 13-48% of the text falls outside the page. Rendered pages confirm the identifying columns are gone: person and ticker on trades, ticker on flows and breadth_yoy, date/source/seed on sentinels.

The AMBER PDFs are the right view but degraded: unwrapped cells overprint neighbours on oge (34%), disagreements (33%), member (13%), insiders (7%) and portfolios (2-3%); the member PDF is titled "Member fusion - ?"; the ticker PDF omits the three-surface tension table shown on screen.

The other 12 CSV exports return page and whole-dataset row counts equal to the on-screen totals, with zero impossible dates in any export.

Probes and saved PDFs/text are in ~/probes/aud1/ on basilic (d_explore1.py, d_probe_main.py, d_probe2.py, d_probe3.sh, d_probe4.py, d_out/). One scratch file of mine (d_out/repro.tmp) was created and removed there; nothing else on the host was changed.

### D - evidence

**D24 - RED - PDF: /breadth_yoy**

- Evidence: 200, brief_breadth_yoy, 'Breadth change CY2025 vs CY2024', '64 single names ...', 2 pages, no front-brief marker. The table is 22 columns x 0.5in = 11in, centred on an 8.5in page: text reaches x=719 on a 612pt page and 656 of 5,198 glyphs (12.6%) fall outside it. Rendered page 1 shows no ticker or instrument column (off the left edge); new_low_conf and exited_low_conf are off the right. Also prints raw Python values ("['JT']", "{'ST': 45}") and years as '2,019'. Cause: render_page_brief sets colWidths = max(0.5in, 7.2in/ncols), so any view with 18 or more columns overflows.
- Verifier agrees: Reproduced: brief_breadth_yoy, own title 'Breadth change CY2025 vs CY2024', 2 pages, no front-brief marker; text x-range -84..719 on a 612pt page, 680 of 5,198 glyphs (13.1%) off page. Cropping to the page, 'BAC' (the first ticker) is absent. Render shows no ticker column and raw "['JT']" / "{'ST': 45}" cells. Cause confirmed in brief.py: w = max(0.5in, 7.2in/len(cols)) with 22 columns.
- Proposed order: Make render_page_brief never emit a table wider than the frame (landscape, a print column subset, or wrapped cells) and fail loudly if one would be; add a test asserting no glyph lies outside the page for every printable view.

**D25 - RED - PDF: /committees**

- Evidence: /committees print link -> 200, 'Committee holdings - all committees', rows HSPW/HSAP/HSAS: correct for the roll. /committees?cmte=HSPW (screen: 207 tickers, first row AAPL 9 holders) emits print link /brief.pdf?...&view=committees with no cmte, and the PDF returned is again 'Committee holdings - all committees' with committee rows, under filename brief_committees. Hand-built /brief.pdf?view=committees&cmte=HSPW returns the right content ('Committee holdings - HSPW', AAPL SH 9 544,506), so the handler works and the link is the defect (_qs() has no cmte key). Same class as J6: a button that downloads another page's content.
- Verifier agrees: Reproduced. /committees?cmte=HSPW (screen: AAPL first, 207 total) emits print link ...&view=committees with no cmte; that PDF is 'Committee holdings - all committees' with rows HSPW / HSAP and no AAPL. From cmte=HSPW&page=2 (screen first VBR) the button prints page 2 of the roll (HSAS29, SSGA). Hand-built /brief.pdf?view=committees&cmte=HSPW returns 'Committee holdings - HSPW', AAPL SH 9 544,506.
- Proposed order: Same fix as D10 (carry cmte in _qs()); extend the J6 test to assert the drill-down's print link yields a PDF titled for that committee.

**D34 - RED - CSV: /clusters.csv?which=buy**

- Evidence: From the page's own links and 22 further variants (ctf 7/30/90/180/365, floor 5/10, capit=1, sort, page, per_page), every which=buy request -> 500 'ValueError: CSV contract violation: ['unmapped'] present on a row but neither exported nor listed in omit'. All 354 buy rows carry key 'unmapped' (16 truthy); it is not in _CLUSTER_BUY_COLS and no omit is declared. The key was added to cluster rows in 883baf7 (2026-09-22); the contract check dates from 0f93a90 (2026-08-17). tests/test_dashboard.py:173 only asserts the header on its fixture. The dashboard log held no /clusters.csv 500 before this audit's 28.
- Verifier agrees: Reproduced on 8 requests: page link, whole-dataset link, capit/ctf variant and bare /clusters.csv (which defaults to buy) all -> 500 'ValueError: CSV contract violation: ['unmapped'] present on a row but neither exported nor listed in omit' while the screen shows 354 buy clusters. _CLUSTER_BUY_COLS (dashboard.py:2047) has no 'unmapped'; queries.py:1475 sets it on every cluster row; git show 883baf7 (2026-09-22) adds that key, 0f93a90 (2026-08-17) added the contract check; tests/test_dashboard.py:173 only asserts startswith(header). Log: 40 /clusters.csv 500s, all after the last start banner, all from the host's own address (probes; 8 are mine). Request lines carry no timestamp, so 'none before the audit' rests on position relative to that banner.
- Proposed order: Add 'unmapped' to _CLUSTER_BUY_COLS (or declare it omitted with a reason) and add a test that exports a non-empty buy-cluster list.

### D - found by the verifier, missed by the auditor

- Print from a later page was never tested. On /congress, /congress_gaps and /oge the PDF ignores the page parameter and always prints the first 200 rows of the whole set (render_page_brief max_rows=200; _view_spec does no page slice for portfolios, insiders, oge, congress_gaps, breadth_yoy, disagreements, congress). From /congress?page=5, /congress_gaps?page=5 and /oge?page=4 the 'Print page (PDF)' button returns a PDF that does not contain the first row on screen (FTNT, 'Kim, Andy', line 46 'THSDFS LLC'). 1,672 of 1,872 breadth rows, 716 of 916 coverage rows and 162 of 362 OGE lines cannot be printed at all. The PDF does say 'Showing the first 200 of N rows ... truncated for print, not filtered'. Trades, sentinels, flows, member, committees and clusters do honour page. Proposed order: slice the print spec by page like the other six views, or relabel the button and state the row range in the subtitle.
- The bare /ticker page (no symbol) renders a 'Print page (PDF)' button whose link answers HTTP 400. The auditor recorded the 400 under D31 but not that a visible button leads to it; dashboard.py:112-115 gates the button on the path alone, against its own comment 'Offered only where there is something of THIS view to print'.
- The /congress breadth PDF prints 12 columns where the screen and CSV have 16 (self, spouse, joint, dependent are dropped) with no note saying so, although the _view_spec docstring says PDF, screen and CSV use the same column constants.
- /breadth_yoy.csv carries Python reprs in cells ('[]', "{'ST': 45}"); the auditor noted this only for the PDF.
- Every /brief.pdf request leaves a directory and a PDF behind in the host temp dir: _brief calls tempfile.mkdtemp() and never removes it. At 19:36 there were 97 leftover brief.pdf files under /var/folders/.../T, nearly all from audit probes (roughly 60 from mine, as a side effect of GET requests). A slow leak, not a view failure; worth an order.
- dashboard.err.log is 176 MB and 2,836,040 lines with no rotation: 134,931 'OSError: [Errno 49] Can't assign requested address' tracebacks from past bind crash loops (59 start banners, old ones under ~/Code/Abelard) and 4 BrokenPipe. Request lines have no timestamp because log_message drops it, so the auditor's 'no /clusters.csv 500 before this audit' can only be read as 'none before the last start banner'. Since that banner: 1,420 requests, all from the host's own address, 0 tracebacks, 40 500s, every one /clusters.csv. Probably belongs to another section, but it bounds what the log can prove here.
- D11/D32 call window=3650 'all-time'. It is ten years: the export starts 2017-03-31 while the footer puts the corpus back to 2002. No all-time export was exercised.
- The /congress/member/<id> path form is not in ROUTES and has no print button, so the 'Member book' for a member reached by that URL cannot be printed from that page (the auditor noted the missing button but drew no finding).

### D - verifier's verdict

I re-ran every one of the 46 rows myself, not a sample, and 44 hold. Two GREENs do not: D18 (/congress PDF) and D23 (/congress_gaps PDF) should be AMBER. Corrected tally: 30 GREEN, 9 AMBER, 7 RED.

**Why D18 and D23 drop.** The auditor only printed from page 1. On /congress, /congress_gaps and /oge the PDF ignores the page parameter and always prints the first 200 rows. From page 5 of either congress view, the print button returns a PDF that does not contain the rows on screen. The PDF states the truncation, so this is AMBER, not RED. D20 (/oge) has the same gap but was already AMBER.

**All seven REDs reproduce.**
- **Buy-cluster CSV (D34):** every `which=buy` request, and bare `/clusters.csv`, returns HTTP 500 with the `unmapped` contract violation while the screen shows 354 clusters.
- **Committee drill-down (D25, D44; AMBER D10):** none of the 23 links on `/committees?cmte=HSPW` carries `cmte`, so print and both CSV links return the all-committees roll. Hand-typed `cmte` works.
- **PDFs off the page (D24, D26, D27, D29):** 13% to 48% of glyphs fall outside the 612pt page. Rendered pages show no ticker on breadth_yoy and flows, no person or ticker on trades, and no date, source or seed on sentinels.

**The rest holds.**
- **Routes:** all 15 answer 200 with populated tables or explicit empty-state sentences, and 835 same-view links followed returned no non-200.
- **J6:** has not recurred. All 14 printable views carry their own title and no front-brief text, and five non-printable requests answered 400.
- **CSVs:** the other 13 exports return page and whole-dataset counts equal to the on-screen totals.
- **Footer:** matches the auditor's quote word for word and reconciles exactly against my own read-only SQL.

**Overprint AMBERs** (D17, D19–D22) are confirmed; my overlap measure is higher than the auditor's on oge and disagreements (about 51–52% against 33–34%), which makes those two borderline RED.

One small gap in D15/D31: the bare /ticker page shows a print button that answers 400.

The service runs as PID 16947 from abelard-live at c3b7dbb, started 16:31:33, and the tree was clean after every probe. I wrote only under `~/probes/aud1/` (`vd_*.py`, `vd_*.sh`, `vd_out/`). The dashboard itself leaves a temp PDF per print request, so my GETs added about 60 files to the host temp dir.

## E - Data-quality state

Section E measured read-only on basilic between 19:07 and 19:20 EDT on 2026-10-01: 2 GREEN, 5 AMBER, 4 RED across 11 rows (E1-E7 plus four E8 findings).

RED:
- E2: placeholder tickers are not zero on display. The cluster board is clean, but the live /clusters sell feed renders issuers named NONE and N/A, and the ownership-pressure board names one issuer '[NONE]' at rank 377. Stored: 2,493 Form 4 rows on 217 issuers.

**E8a - RED - Senate PTRs re-filed without an amendment label are counted twice**

- Evidence: Grouping live rows (superseded=0) on person, tx_date, ticker, side, amount band, owner and asset_name: 1,402 Senate groups appear in more than one filing, 1,495 surplus rows. They span 107 filing sets and 194 filings; none carries an 'amend' report_label and all have filing_status NULL. 50 two-filing sets are at least 90% identical wholesale and cover 1,150 of the groups, e.g. Perdue filings 75f0461a (2016-08-13) and 5bdbba1a (2016-09-01), 127 rows each, trades 2015-01-06 to 2015-05-29, both fully live. Stock rows with a ticker only: 926 groups, 966 surplus of 9,044 (10.7%). Surplus by disclosure year: 2014 186; 2015 243; 2016 210; 2018 246; 2020 205; 2023 104; 2024 86. House equivalent: 24 groups, 26 surplus. Only the ticker panel annotates these ('POTENTIAL AMENDMENT'); every other reader of superseded=0 counts them twice. Identical rows inside one filing (House 751, Senate 728 surplus) are excluded here; amendments.py records those as genuine.
- Verifier agrees: Reproduced with my own grouping (person, tx_date, ticker, side, amt_low, amt_high, owner, asset_name on superseded=0): Senate 14,146 live rows, 1,402 groups appearing in more than one filing, 1,495 surplus counted as (distinct filings - 1) = 10.6%; 194 filings involved; every one of the 3,086 rows has filing_status NULL; 0 of the filings carry an 'amend' label. Surplus by year of the earliest filing matches the auditor exactly (2014 186; 2015 243; 2016 210; 2018 246; 2020 205; 2023 104; 2024 86). Perdue filings 75f0461a (2016-08-13) and 5bdbba1a (2016-09-01): 127 rows each, all live, trades 2015-01-06 to 2015-05-29. House: 24 groups, 26 surplus. Small differences that do not move the verdict: counted as rows beyond the fullest filing the surplus is 1,588; stock-with-ticker I get 934 groups / 976 surplus of 9,044 against the auditor's 926 / 966 (10.8% vs 10.7%); with my definition of wholesale (>= 90% of the larger filing's distinct keys) it is 40 filing pairs covering 1,241 groups against the auditor's 50 / 1,150. Caveat the row should carry: this is a documented choice, not an oversight - annotate_potential_amendments says doctrine forbids collapsing on resemblance alone and it has exactly one call site (the ticker panel), so the order needed is a ruling.
- Proposed order: Decide a supersede rule for Senate filings that restate another filing wholesale, then recompute superseded.

**E4 - GREEN - Mojibake count (smart_money.mojibake.scan_mojibake)**

Non-ASCII elsewhere is legitimate (congress_holdings.asset_name 62,714, congress_trades.comment 75, roster names 5), 0 suspected. - E4: the scanner's regex only detects UTF-8 mis-read as Latin-1. Mis-reads through cp1252 (the root cause its own docstring names) produce characters such as U+20AC and U+2122 that it cannot match. A cp1252-aware sweep of every text column finds nothing extra today, so the count of 1 stands, but the instrument would under-count if such rows arrived.
- E5: 3 unflagged congress rows are dated one day after their own disclosure_date (House Government Security 2024-01-25 vs 2024-01-24; Senate Stock 2017-06-02 vs 2017-06-01; Senate WFC 2016-12-23 vs 2016-12-22). - E8b: 28 persons of type 'insider' also have names longer than 60 characters; not examined.
