**F12 - GREEN - Universal backfill - bounded backfill of universal Form 4 daily indexes for 2026-09-04..09-21**

- Evidence: form4_universal_days has a row for every weekday 2026-09-04..09-21 except 09-07 (Labor Day): 11 days, each completed the following night at 22:3x, parse_fail=0 (e.g. 09-04: 441 filings / 774 rows; 09-17: 695 / 1,522; 09-21: 385 / 684). Scan envelopes exist for every night in the window (scan_*.json 2026-09-04 through 09-22, edgar_form4_universal status OK each night). Across the table's whole range (2025-07-23..2026-09-29, 296 days) the only missing weekdays are 14 US holiday dates plus 09-30, which tonight's scan ingests. Spot check against EDGAR's own form.idx fetched read-only today: 2026-09-10 has 508 distinct Form 4 accessions (stored form4_count 508, all 508 in form4_backfill_seen); 2026-09-17 has 695 (stored 695, all 695 seen). Mechanism: form4_universal.ingest_recent walks a 7-day lookback each night and fills any day not watermarked. Latent hazard seen in passing: form4.daily_form4 returns None for any non-200 response, so a throttled or blocked index is indistinguishable from a holiday, and a miss older than 7 days would never be retried or reported.
- Verifier agrees: Confirmed on more evidence than the auditor used. form4_universal_days has all 11 weekdays 09-04..09-21 (09-07 absent), each completed the next night at 22:32-22:36, parse_fail 0. A scan envelope exists for every night 08-20..09-30, with edgar_form4_universal OK each time. I fetched EDGAR's daily index for four other days: 09-04 = 441, 09-08 = 484, 09-15 = 504, 09-21 = 385 distinct Form 4 accessions, each equal to the stored form4_count and each 100% present in form4_backfill_seen. 09-07, 2025-12-24 and 2025-12-26 return no index, consistent with EDGAR closures. Whole-range missing weekdays: the same 14 dates plus 09-30. No backfill code was written and none is needed. The latent hazard is real: daily_form4 returns None on any non-200, and ingest_recent then skips the day silently; beyond the 7-day lookback it is never retried.
- Proposed order: Close the universal-backfill order as moot. Optional small order: make a non-200 daily index a counted DEGRADED rather than a silent skip.

(2) deploy_live.sh sits in the tree it merges and has no run-from-a-copy guard; the sibling daemons/capex_daemon/scripts/deploy_live.sh documents that exact hazard as having 'bit this gate twice' and guards against it. (5) post_deploy.sh echoes 'restarted' without checking that the dashboard answers. (6) NOT VERIFIED: 'fails loud if the nightly scan holds the write lock' - db.connect shows no explicit busy_timeout and testing it needs a writing connection, which this audit forbids. PRAGMA user_version = 0, so the database does not record its schema level.
- Verifier agrees: Read both scripts in full (hv4.sh). scripts/deploy_live.sh: -rwxr-xr-x, index mode 100755, 39 lines, `sh -n` OK, last changed 3271a71 (2026-09-22 21:10:42). It does what its header says and what E33's gate corollary requires: cd to own tree; REFUSE unless branch = main; REFUSE on non-empty `status --porcelain --untracked-files=no`; note (not fail) on untracked; `git fetch origin`; `git merge --ff-only origin/main`; REFUSE unless HEAD == origin/main AFTER the merge; print before -> after and the log; no redirects; set -eu. post_deploy.sh: -rwxr-xr-x, 100755, 25 lines, `bash -n` OK, new in c3b7dbb; cd to daemon dir; `.venv/bin/python` runs `db.connect(db.DB_PATH_DEFAULT)` + close; `launchctl kickstart -k gui/$(id -u)/com.abelard.smart-money-dash`; set -euo pipefail. Traces of today's run: FETCH_HEAD and reflog both 16:31:33 (fetch + merge back-to-back, as the script does); db.cpython-314.pyc 16:31:33.818 then dashboard start 16:31:33.898; launchctl status -15, runs = 4; read-only PRAGMA table_info shows date_flag/date_subclass/tx_date_suggested on congress_trades, form4_transactions, form4_derivatives and filed_date_raw on oge_holdings; user_version 0; SMART_MONEY_DB_PATH in the daemon .env = the canonical ~/.openclaw path. All six auditor caveats check out: comment on line 19 calls consensus/data live-root state when it is a symlink; no run-from-copy guard although the capex sibling (lines 28-45) documents that hazard; two different gates share the name; the root gate neither calls nor mentions post_deploy.sh and restarts nothing; post_deploy echoes 'restarted' unverified. AMBER holds. Refinements: (a) the unverified 'fails loud under a held write lock' claim is at least consistent with the code - db.connect uses `sqlite3.connect(str(p))` with Python's default 5 s busy timeout, so a write blocked longer raises OperationalError and set -e aborts BEFORE the kickstart; still static reading, not a test. (b) 'additive' is true of the c3b7dbb delta only: connect() also runs executescript(SCHEMA) and older guarded UPDATE backfills. (c) the root gate runs no tests, whereas the capex gate does (line 90) - a substantive difference the auditor listed only implicitly. (d) the traces cannot distinguish the script from the same two git commands typed by hand.
- Proposed order: Give the root gate the run-from-copy guard; have it call (or at least name) each daemon's post_deploy hook and poll the dashboards; retire or subordinate the capex-specific gate; correct the consensus/data comment.

### H - verifier's verdict

All seven RAGs hold: 2 GREEN, 5 AMBER, 0 RED, 0 UNKNOWN. I re-ran the decisive evidence for every row myself (probes ~/probes/aud1/hv1.sh to hv6.sh, 19:19-19:26 EDT) and the auditor's numbers reproduce almost exactly.

The two GREENs are well-founded. H1 rests on three independent readings (local ref, a live ls-remote returning c3b7dbb21ab7, and a 0/0 ahead/behind count). H2 rests on a full re-hash of all 789 tracked files, not on git's stat cache; I got the same single eol-normalisation mismatch and the same byte-identical cmp.

The central provenance question - is anything running old code - is answered NO with strong evidence: the smart_money dashboard started 16:31:33.898, after the last c3b7dbb file landed at 16:31:33.311, and serves the `date_note` column that does not exist anywhere in d135a1d. capex-dash predates today's deploy but c3b7dbb touched only smart_money_daemon.

Corrections to the auditor's work:
1. H6: 'the dev venv appears unused since Jul 22' is wrong. atime is not maintained on this volume, and _sync_snapshot.db was created at 19:09:52 today, so sync_from_basilic.sh's remote step (which runs the dev checkout's interpreter) ran today.
2. H6/H4: the dev checkout hosts production in four ways, not two - the 22 GB consensus corpus and logs (reported), the dev venv used by the sync (reported as a residue), plus the live root's entire git repository (it is a linked worktree of ~/Code/Abelard/.git) and a news_watch .env path into dev's doctrine/THESES.md (both unreported). H6 stays AMBER because the row's literal test (no plist names it) passes and code provenance is intact, but it is the row closest to RED and the proposed order should cover all four.
3. H3: the nightly-scan plist has no WorkingDirectory and runs through a login shell; H1: the reflog has six worktree-creation entries, not only fast-forwards; H5: 42 files, not 43, at the local branch tip. None changes a RAG.
4. H7: the one claim the auditor left unverified (fails loud under a held write lock) is at least consistent with the code via Python's default 5 s sqlite timeout plus set -e; it remains untested.

One open hygiene question for the orchestrator: a 665 MB VACUUM INTO snapshot was written into the smart_money state home at 19:09:52, inside the audit window, through a non-read-only connection. I could not attribute it.

My own footprint: six probe scripts in ~/probes/aud1/, GETs to /, /health and /sentinels.csv on 8787 and /health on 8788 (access-log lines), one read-only git ls-remote. No database opened except mode=ro; all four worktrees re-checked with identical status afterwards (live 0/0, dev 0 tracked + 9 untracked, fdu 0 + 1, smjan2 0/0). No secret values printed (variable names only).

## I - Q3 window readiness

Q3 readiness is RED on the two things that matter: no arrival ledger exists in any form, and the live disagreement board already mixes quarters without showing it. The 13F ingest itself is healthy (10 of 10 recent nightly scans OK, 28 filers, 0 errors), and nobody has filed Q3 yet, as expected on day 1.

- **Ledger (I1, I2):** there is no arrival table, module or test on main or any branch. The code reads only form `13F-HR`. All four arrival states are live in the roster today and only one is represented: Pershing Square filed a 13F-NT for Q2 on 2026-08-14 that the system cannot see, Thiel Macro has two filed-empty quarters stored as accessions with no period, and Founders Fund VII has filed nothing since February.
- **Roster (I3):** 28 filers; 25 are on 2026-06-30, and Pershing, Founders Fund VII and Scion are behind. EDGAR was checked for three filers only (Pershing, Thiel Macro, Founders Fund VII); none has a Q3 filing.
- **Arrival curve (I4):** for Q2, 0 filers by day 30, 3 by day 40, 6 by day 44, 25 by day 45, with 76% on the deadline day. Expect first Q3 arrivals around 11-03 to 11-05 and the bulk on Monday 11-16.
- **Queries (I5):** "newest period" is whatever period last has rows, per filer, and the prior need not be the adjacent quarter. Today 12 of 80 opposed-pair rows mix quarters, and the page and CSV omit the per-side period that the docstring says is visible. A replay of the Q2 window gives 27-28% mixed rows, with about half the mid-window board gone by deadline day.
- **Build order (I6):** rulings, then the ledger, then the reader cohort change, then a replay proof, then B2. The practical target is about 2026-11-02, not 11-16. B2 cannot be sized because no B2 or SIDE SWAPS spec is on the host, and JAN2 status was not measured here.
- **Extra (I7):** the filing countdown uses 45 calendar days, so it will say "filing window passed" from Saturday 11-14, two days before the real deadline.

Nothing was changed. The only writes were 12 probe scripts in `~/probes/aud1/` (local copies in `C:\tmp\aud1_I\`). The database was opened `mode=ro` only; that moves the `-shm` file's timestamp, not the database. Three read-only EDGAR requests were made using the daemon's own User-Agent, with the contact value never printed. `~/Code/Abelard-smjan2` was not touched.

| ID | RAG | Item | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| I1 | **RED** | Does any arrival ledger exist (table, module, test)? | No. There is no arrival table, module or test in the live tree (main d135a1d) or on any other local or remote branch. The only mentions of 13F-NT are prose in manager_resolve.py and its one-off report, which nothing in the scan or query path uses. |
| I2 | **RED** | What thirteenf_filings_seen and list_13f_filings record; which of the four states each can represent; other form types | Both see only form == '13F-HR'. Only filed-with-rows is represented honestly: filed-empty survives as an orphan accession with no period, and awaited and NT-notice cannot be represented at all. Nothing reads 13F-NT or 13F-HR/A, so Pershing Square's Q2 13F-NT (filed 2026-08-14) is invisible while the nightly scan reports that filer OK. |
| I3 | **AMBER** | CONFIRMED filer set: count, newest period and filed_date per filer; has anyone filed for 2026-09-30 | 28 filers, matching the 28 registry manager_13f entries. Nobody has filed Q3 yet, which is expected one day after quarter end, and the nightly ingest has run clean for the last ten nights. AMBER because 3 of 28 are not on Q2 and the database cannot say why; EDGAR was checked for only 3 filers. |
| I4 | **GREEN** | Arrival curve for period 2026-06-30 (by day 30, 40, 45) and what it predicts for the 2026-11-16 deadline | Measured. For Q2, none of the 25 filers with rows had filed by day 30, 3 by day 40, 6 by day 44, and all 25 by day 45; 19 of 25 (76%) filed on the deadline day itself. The three earlier quarters have the same shape, so expect the first Q3 arrivals around 11-03 to 11-05, a handful by Friday 11-13, and roughly three quarters on Monday 11-16. |
| I5 | **RED** | What opposed-pairs / convergence need from an arrival ledger to be honest during a filing window; how they pick the newest period per filer | Each filer's newest period is simply the latest period that has rows in thirteenf_holdings, and the prior is the next one down, adjacent or not. The live board already mixes quarters on 12 of 80 rows and does not show the period per side; a replay of the Q2 window shows 27-28% mixed rows and about half the mid-window board gone by deadline day. Convergence groups by (ticker, period) with no statement of how many filers have reported. |
| I6 | **AMBER** | What must be built, in what order, for B2 to exist before 2026-11-16 | Feasible but nothing exists yet, and the usable window is shorter than the deadline suggests: the board starts mixing Q3 with Q2 from the first arrival around 11-03 to 11-05, so the ledger and reader changes need to be live by about 11-02. The ledger half is small; the reader half carries the risk; B2 itself cannot be sized from the host because no B2 or SIDE SWAPS spec text is in the live tree. |
| I7 | **AMBER** | Extra finding: the days-to-next-filing countdown ignores the weekend/holiday roll | The tracked-books strip counts down to quarter end + 45 calendar days. For Q3 that is Saturday 2026-11-14, two days before the real deadline of Monday 2026-11-16, so 25 cards will read 'filing window passed' while most filers have not yet filed and are not late. |

### I - evidence

**I1 - RED - Does any arrival ledger exist (table, module, test)?**

- Evidence: ~/probes/aud1/i1_grep.sh on ~/Code/abelard-live/daemons/smart_money_daemon (96 .py files, tests/ included): `grep -rIc -i arrival` -> no hits; `grep -i awaited` -> no hits; `grep -E '13F-NT\|NT 13F\|NT-notice\|nt_notice'` -> only manager_resolve.py:9,10,38,107 and scans/SM_P2_FILER_RESOLUTION.md:5,11,55. `filings_seen` -> thirteenf_ingest.py:185,234; db.py:319; scan.py:458 (plus an unrelated stats key in two reparse tools). i1b_refs.sh: `git --no-optional-locks grep -l -i -E 'arrival\|awaited\|13F-NT\|NT-notice\|nt_notice' <ref> -- daemons/smart_money_daemon` over main, ps-1-price-substrate, fdu-pa1-recon and their origin/ refs -> nothing beyond those two files. Live DB (mode=ro) has 28 tables; the 13F ones are thirteenf_baseline 28 rows, thirteenf_filing_meta 225, thirteenf_filings_seen 230, thirteenf_holdings 13697. grep for 'SM-H1\|SIDE SWAP\|SM-B2' in the live tree -> 0 hits under smart_money_daemon.
- Verifier agrees: Re-ran my own grep (~/probes/aud1/vI_1.sh, vI_2.sh) on ~/Code/abelard-live/daemons/smart_money_daemon at main d135a1d: 96 .py files; `arrival` 0 hits; `awaited` 0 hits; 13F-NT only in smart_money/manager_resolve.py:9,10,38,107 and scans/SM_P2_FILER_RESOLUTION.md (prose). `filings_seen` only at thirteenf_ingest.py:185,234, db.py:319, scan.py:458 plus the two unrelated reparse stats keys. Whole live repo: 0 hits for SM-H1 / SIDE SWAP / SM-B2; every other `arrival` hit is in consensus/ or capex_daemon and unrelated. Live DB opened mode=ro: 28 tables, no arrival/ledger table, no sqlite_master SQL containing arrival or awaited; thirteenf_baseline 28, thirteenf_filing_meta 225, thirteenf_filings_seen 230, thirteenf_holdings 13697 - all match. I went one step beyond the auditor's ref-only check: a filesystem grep of the three other worktrees, including the uncommitted files in ~/Code/Abelard-smjan2 (read-only, nothing touched), finds the same two files and nothing else, and the uncommitted diff there has 0 changed lines touching 13F-HR/13F-NT/_filer_periods/filings_seen/CREATE TABLE. So no ledger exists anywhere on the host, committed or not. RED (missing) holds.
- Proposed order: SM-H1: build the arrival ledger (table + writer + backfill + tests). See I6 for scope and order.

**I5 - RED - What opposed-pairs / convergence need from an arrival ledger to be honest during a filing window; how they pick the newest period per filer**

- Evidence: queries.py:1613 _filer_periods = `SELECT DISTINCT period FROM thirteenf_holdings ... ORDER BY period DESC`; _manager_flow (1879-1883) uses periods[0] vs periods[1]; q_opposed_pairs (1943) pools every filer's flows whatever their period. phase4_joins.join_d (232-236) pairs consecutive HELD periods; join_a groups on (ticker, period). i5_replay.py live: (newest, prior) = 23 filers 2026-06-30 vs 2026-03-31; Thiel Macro 2026-06-30 vs 2025-09-30, flows {'new': 8, 'exited': 3}, across two filed-empty quarters; Founders Fund Growth II 2026-06-30 vs 2025-12-31; Pershing 2026-03-31 vs 2025-12-31 (9 flows, though it filed a 13F-NT for Q2); Scion 2025-09-30 vs 2025-06-30 (21 flows, a year old); Founders Fund VII 2025-12-31 vs 2025-09-30. `[LIVE 2026-10-01] opposed-pair rows=80 filers_compared=27 mixed-period rows=12 (15%)`: 7 rows mix 2026-03-31 with 2026-06-30, 5 mix 2025-09-30 with 2026-06-30, e.g. ('GOOGL','SH', Pershing, '2026-03-31', 'trimmed'). The docstring says the period is reported per side, but dashboard.py:530-536 renders only ticker, instr, counts, names and values, and _DIS_CSV_COLS has no period. Replay (in-process patch of _filer_periods to filed_date<=D, nothing written; a replay of the query logic, not of what was shown at the time): D=08-10, 3 filers on Q2 and 21 on Q1 -> 88 rows, 24 mixed (27%); D=08-13 -> 90 rows, 25 mixed (28%); of the 08-13 board, 45 pairs survive to 08-14 and 45 vanish, and 35 of the final 80 are not yet shown. Convergence live: 1111 rows over 9 periods, 138 for 2026-06-30 on 25 of 28 filers, coverage not stated. A filer that files empty or NT for Q3 keeps its Q2 book as newest, so a full liquidation never registers as exits.
- Verifier agrees: Read queries.py: _filer_periods (1613) is SELECT DISTINCT period FROM thirteenf_holdings ORDER BY period DESC; _manager_flow (1860, periods read at 1879) compares periods[0] with periods[1] with no adjacency test; q_opposed_pairs (1924) pools all filers' flows keyed (ticker, instrument); phase4_joins.join_d pairs consecutive held periods; join_a groups by (ticker, period). Re-ran against the live DB, mode=ro (vI_9.py): `[LIVE] rows=80 filers_compared=27 mixed=12 (15%)`, combos 7 x (2026-03-31, 2026-06-30) and 5 x (2025-09-30, 2026-06-30). Non-adjacent priors: Thiel Macro 2026-06-30 vs 2025-09-30 (8 new, 3 exited); FF Growth II 2026-06-30 vs 2025-12-31, across the Q1 filing I confirmed is filed-empty. Pershing 9 flows from 2026-03-31; Scion 21 flows from 2025-09-30; Founders Fund VII 0 flows, which is why 27 not 28. My own in-process as-of replay (filed_date <= D, nothing written): 08-10 -> 88 rows, 24 mixed (27%); 08-13 -> 90 rows, 25 mixed (28%); of the 08-13 board 45 survive and 45 vanish, 35 of the final 80 not yet shown; the 08-14 replay reproduces the live board exactly. dashboard.py view_disagreements (520-536) renders ticker, instr, counts, names, values and no period; _DIS_CSV_COLS has no period and _DIS_CSV_OMIT drops the per-side lists. The page text does say sides 'can be slightly off-phase', but a row never shows which side or by how much. Convergence: 1111 rows, 9 periods, 138 for 2026-06-30, and its note says '13F universe = 28 registry CIKs' while only 25 have Q2 rows. All figures match; RED holds. One thing to add to the proposed reader: a ledger state of NT-notice is not enough on its own to be honest about Pershing, because its holdings continue under another CIK (I3).
- Proposed order: SM-H1 reader half: one cohort function fed by the ledger. Fix the target quarter; only filers whose ledger state for it is filed contribute a flow; the prior must be the adjacent quarter (filed-empty prior means every position is new; awaited or NT prior means no direction). Show 'k of N reported' plus the awaited and NT names, and render the period per side in the HTML, CSV and brief.

Reader call sites to change: queries.py:1491 (q_surface_tension), 1763 (q_portfolio), 1879 (_manager_flow), 2002 (q_tracked_books), phase4_joins.join_d; renderers dashboard.py:490, 520, 557, 607, 2165 and brief.py:79. JAN2 status was not measured in this section; ~/Code/Abelard-smjan2 is at detached d135a1d, same as main, and was not touched. grep 'SM-H1\|SIDE SWAP\|SM-B2' -> 0 hits in smart_money_daemon.

No SM-H1 / SM-B2 / SIDE SWAPS text on the host in any worktree, and none in the dev checkout on the Windows machine either, so B2 is unsized everywhere I can read. AMBER holds with three qualifications. (1) 'Feasible' is supported only for the ledger and reader halves; whether B2 itself can exist by 11-16 is UNKNOWN until a spec is written, and the row should not be read as a yes. (2) An unnamed dependency: the uncommitted change in ~/Code/Abelard-smjan2 (read-only diff --stat) modifies exactly the files SM-H1 must change - queries.py +245 lines, phase4_joins.py, db.py +27, scan.py, thirteenf_ingest.py +9, dashboard.py - so every cited line number moves once it lands and SM-H1 must branch after it. Saying that tree is 'at d135a1d, same as main' is true of HEAD only. (3) The rulings list is missing the Pershing roster question: add CIK 0002026053 or drop Pershing; CONFIRMED carries 'no additions without an order'. The first-arrival date is better stated as 11-04 to 11-07 (I4); the 11-02 target still stands.
- Proposed order: After JAN2 closes. (1) Owner rulings: meaning of 13F-NT on the board, dormant filers (Scion, Founders Fund VII), whether 13F-HR/A replaces holdings, and the written B2 spec. (2) SM-H1a ledger: one table keyed (cik, period) with state filed_rows / filed_empty / nt_notice / awaited, form, accession, filed_date, first_seen, deadline with weekend and holiday roll; writer inside the nightly 13F leg reading the unfiltered feed; one-time backfill of the 230 seen accessions, the 5 orphan empties and NT history (28 GETs); tests for all four states, amendments and idempotence; scan status that names NT and awaited instead of OK. Small: one module, one table, no new dependency; needs a DB snapshot before migration. (3) SM-H1b readers: the cohort function and adjacent-quarter rule from I5 across the five call sites and three renderers, with existing tests updated. Medium, and the main risk. (4) Prove it on the Q2 window by as-of replay before Q3 arrives. (5) B2 harvest block + SIDE SWAPS on top of the cohort function: size unknown until the spec exists. Target live for (2)-(4): by 2026-11-02.

**I7 - AMBER - Extra finding: the days-to-next-filing countdown ignores the weekend/holiday roll**

- Evidence: queries.py:1729 _days_to_next_13f: `due = _next_quarter_end(latest_period) + dt.timedelta(days=45)`. i5c.py: q_tracked_books(anchor=2026-10-01) -> {('2026-06-30', 44): 25, ('2026-03-31', -48): 1, ('2025-12-31', -139): 1, ('2025-09-30', -229): 1}; `2026-09-30 + 45d = 2026-11-14 Saturday`; `2026-11-16 is Monday = day 47`. Precedent in the data: 2025-12-31 + 45d = Saturday 2026-02-14, and 15 filers filed on Tuesday 2026-02-17 (day 48). dashboard.py:459 renders d2f < 0 as 'filing window passed'. This negative countdown is the only arrival signal the system has today, and it reads the same for Pershing (NT filed), Founders Fund VII (nothing filed) and Scion.
- Verifier agrees: queries.py:1729 `due = _next_quarter_end(latest_period) + dt.timedelta(days=45)` with no business-day roll; dashboard.py:459 renders d2f < 0 as 'filing window passed'. Re-ran q_tracked_books(anchor='2026-10-01') read-only: {(2026-06-30, 44): 25, (2026-03-31, -48): 1, (2025-12-31, -139): 1, (2025-09-30, -229): 1} - matches. 2026-09-30 + 45d = Saturday 2026-11-14; Monday 2026-11-16 is day 47. Precedent holds: 2025-12-31 + 45d = Saturday 2026-02-14, and 15 filers filed Tuesday 02-17. AMBER holds. Two small corrections: the test is d2f < 0, so Saturday 11-14 shows '0d to next filing' and the 'filing window passed' label starts Sunday 11-15, not Saturday as the summary says; and it applies only to filers still on Q2, which by the rolled-quarter precedent is nearer 15-19 cards than 25. The nightly ingest runs about 23:10, so the wrong label stays up through the whole of deadline day Monday 11-16.
- Proposed order: Fold into SM-H1a: the ledger owns the deadline per period with the business-day roll, and the strip reads state and deadline from the ledger instead of computing them inline.

**I4 - GREEN - Arrival curve for period 2026-06-30 (by day 30, 40, 45) and what it predicts for the 2026-11-16 deadline**

- Evidence: i3_db.py, days from quarter end to filed_date, CONFIRMED filers with rows. Period 2026-06-30 (25 filers): by day 30 = 0; day 35 = 0; day 40 = 3; day 42 = 5; day 44 = 6; day 45 = 25. By date: 08-05 x1, 08-06 x1, 08-07 x1, 08-11 x2, 08-13 x1, 08-14 x19. Period 2026-03-31 (24): day 40 = 2, day 44 = 7, day 45 = 23, day 50 = 24 (Situational Awareness filed 05-18). Period 2025-12-31 (26; day 45 was Saturday 02-14 and the bulk landed Tuesday 02-17): day 40 = 1, day 44 = 10, day 47 = 10, day 50 = 26; 15 filers on 02-17, Elliott on 02-18. Period 2025-09-30 (27): day 40 = 3, day 44 = 8, day 45 = 27. Earliest lag in any of the four quarters: 34 days. Caveat: the curve covers only 13F-HR filings that produced rows; NT and empty filings are outside it (Pershing's NT and Thiel's empties were all filed on deadline days per EDGAR).
- Verifier agrees: Recomputed from thirteenf_holdings (vI_3.py), min filed_date per filer minus quarter end. 2026-06-30, 25 filers: day 30 = 0, 35 = 0, 40 = 3, 42 = 5, 44 = 6, 45 = 25; by date 08-05 x1, 08-06 x1, 08-07 x1, 08-11 x2, 08-13 x1, 08-14 x19 (76%). 2026-03-31 (24): 40 = 2, 44 = 7, 45 = 23, 50 = 24. 2025-12-31 (26): 40 = 1, 44 = 10, 47 = 10, 50 = 26; 15 on 02-17, 1 on 02-18. 2025-09-30 (27): 40 = 3, 44 = 8, 45 = 27. Every figure matches. No (cik, period) has more than one filed_date, and the DB filed dates I compared with EDGAR (Pershing 05-15, Scion 11-03, FF Growth II 08-14) agree. The measurement is GREEN. Two corrections to the forecast sentence, which is inference from four quarters: (a) the lone day-34 arrival was Scion (2025-11-03), which has filed nothing since; the live early filers are First Eagle, Amazon and Alphabet at day 35-38, so expect first Q3 arrivals 11-04 to 11-07 rather than 11-03. (b) Q3's deadline rolls over a weekend (Sat 11-14 to Mon 11-16), and the only rolled quarter in the data (2025-12-31) had 10 of 26 (38%) in by the Friday before and 15 of 26 (58%) on the rolled deadline day, plus one a day late. So 'a handful by Friday, three quarters on Monday' understates Friday 11-13; roughly a third by Friday and a bit over half on Monday is the better read. Neither changes the conclusion that the board starts mixing in the first week of November.

### I - found by the verifier, missed by the auditor

- Pershing Square's Q2 2026 book exists on EDGAR under a CIK the roster does not track. The 13F-NT (0001172661-26-003777) names PERSHING SQUARE INC., CIK 0002026053, as the reporting manager; that CIK filed 13F-HR 0001172661-26-003790 for 2026-06-30 on 2026-08-14 and has 13F-HR filings back to 2025-06-30. It is not in thirteenf_ingest.CONFIRMED and has 0 rows in the DB. The order asked whether EDGAR holds filings the DB lacks; the auditor found the notice but did not follow its pointer. Without a roster ruling Pershing stays frozen at 2026-03-31 through the Q3 window, and a ledger would only relabel it NT-notice.
- Amendments are already being missed inside the stored window: EDGAR has Pershing 13F-HR/A 0001172661-25-001497 (filed 2025-04-16) for 2024-12-31, and the DB holds only the original 0001172661-25-001119 for that period. The auditor reported '0 amended pairs in the DB', which is true by construction because the code filters /A out; EDGAR was not checked for amendments. I checked only the 3 filers I pulled; the other 25 are unchecked.
- Three of the five orphan accessions were left unchecked by the auditor. I checked one: Founders Fund Growth II 0002106825-26-000003 is a filed-empty 13F-HR for 2026-03-31 (one placeholder row, value 0, tableEntryTotal=1). The two 2020 Thiel Macro orphans (0001315863-20-000924, -20-000698) are still unverified.
- A filed-empty filing arriving in the Q3 window would be reported OK by the nightly scan: the placeholder row makes declared rows (1) differ from parsed rows (0), which lands in report['control_total_mismatch'], but leg_13f_holdings builds its status only from per-filer errors and never reads that key. The auditor said a meta row with parsed_rows=0 would be written but did not trace what the scan status would say.
- The in-flight uncommitted change in ~/Code/Abelard-smjan2 edits the same files SM-H1 must change (queries.py +245 lines, phase4_joins.py, db.py, scan.py, thirteenf_ingest.py, dashboard.py). I6 names 'after JAN2 closes' as the ordering but not this file overlap, and its line references will be stale once that change reaches abelard-live later today.
- JAN2 status, which the order makes the gate for this work, was not measured by the auditor (declared in I6) and I did not measure it either; what remains of JAN2 after today's rollout is UNKNOWN from this section.
- Whether B2 / SIDE SWAPS can exist by 2026-11-16 - the order's direct question - has no evidence-backed answer. No spec text exists on the host or in the dev checkout, so the honest status of that sub-question is UNKNOWN rather than 'feasible'.
- EDGAR coverage for the Q3 question is still partial: 3 roster filers by the auditor and 3 by me (Pershing overlaps), so 5 of 28 checked directly. For the other 23 the evidence that no Q3 filing exists is last night's scan (09-30 23:10, new_filings=0 for all 28), which sees 13F-HR only.

### I - verifier's verdict

All seven rows hold: I re-ran the decisive evidence for each with my own scripts and every number reproduced exactly, so no RAG changes (I1 RED, I2 RED, I3 AMBER, I4 GREEN, I5 RED, I6 AMBER, I7 AMBER).

The one GREEN, the Q2 arrival curve, is a database measurement I recomputed in full and spot-checked against EDGAR filed dates for three filers. Its forecast sentence needs two small corrections. First arrivals are more likely 11-04 to 11-07, because the day-34 filer was Scion, which has since stopped filing. And with the weekend roll, expect about a third in by Friday 11-13 and a bit over half on Monday 11-16.

The auditor's main gap is Pershing Square. Its Q2 13F-NT names Pershing Square Inc. (CIK 0002026053) as the manager reporting its holdings. That CIK filed a 13F-HR for 2026-06-30 on 2026-08-14 and is not in the roster or the database. So the Q2 book is public and missing, and Pershing will sit at 2026-03-31 through the Q3 window unless the owner rules on the roster. An arrival ledger alone does not fix this.

Smaller additions:
- **Amendments:** a real missed 13F-HR/A sits inside the stored window (Pershing, period 2024-12-31).
- **Filed-empty:** the Founders Fund Growth II orphan is confirmed as a filed-empty Q1 2026 filing. A new one in the Q3 window would still be reported OK by the nightly scan.
- **File overlap:** the uncommitted change in `~/Code/Abelard-smjan2` edits the same files SM-H1 must touch, so I6's line references will move once it lands.
- **Countdown:** the "filing window passed" label starts Sunday 11-15, not Saturday 11-14.

On I6, "feasible" is supported only for the ledger and reader work. Whether B2 itself can exist by 11-16 is UNKNOWN: no B2 or SIDE SWAPS spec exists on the host or in the dev checkout, and JAN2 status was not measured.

Nothing was changed. My only writes were 14 probe scripts named `vI_*` in `~/probes/aud1/` (local copies in `C:\tmp\aud1_vI\`). The database was opened `mode=ro` only, git was read-only with `--no-optional-locks`, and `abelard-live` is still clean at d135a1d. I made 8 read-only EDGAR GETs with the daemon's own User-Agent: 4 submissions feeds, one more filer than the order's 2-3 because I followed the notice to the manager it names, and 4 small filing documents. The contact value was never printed.

## Proposed orders, collected

Findings become orders. These are the auditors' proposals, by row.

- **A1** (AMBER): Rule on the DISAGREE_CA class (PRU, SPGI, TMUS in 5 nights) and on the permanent FISV hole so that exit 1 means something new happened; investigate why the 09-23 session was undelivered by both vendors for two nights (451 holes).
- **A2** (GREEN): Make the scan's exit code (or a second status line) reflect any DEGRADED leg, as the prices runner does; name the 36-37 tickers behind the nightly price_fail and rule whether they are expected.
- **A3** (AMBER): Print no_chain in the scan.log line and name the tickers; verify against the vendor whether CTRA and GUTS truly have no chain or are a silent miss; retire or re-map dead symbols (ADGI, CLBR, CMMB) in the overlay.
- **A4** (GREEN): Give run_brief.sh the same `>>>` / `<<< exit N` markers the other runners write, so brief history can be read as exit codes.
- **A5** (GREEN): Add a timestamp to the efd-probe.out line so the log is readable without the database.
- **A7** (GREEN): Rotate or truncate the dashboard error logs (capex 211 MB, smart-money 176 MB).
- **A9** (AMBER): Decide who reads the digest and what drains the queue: 204 items are pending with the consumer disabled since July (A14), and 41 of them are 77 days old.
- **A10** (AMBER): Before 2026-10-21: merge fdu-pa1-recon to main, install an fdu venv under abelard-live and repoint the plist, or renew the exception in writing in doctrine; pass enrich's exit code through run_daily.sh.
- **A11** (AMBER): Run one deliberate inference drill against qwen3:8b; make the runner wait for the Tailscale address (as run_qwen_ui.sh does) instead of crash-looping every 10 s when it is absent.
- **A12** (RED): Repair the Colima instance (vz driver up, host agent not) and bring open-webui back; make run_qwen_ui.sh exit non-zero when colima or the container fails, so the job cannot show running with nothing behind the port.
- **A13** (AMBER): Either remove the plist or record in doctrine that it is parked; if it is ever re-enabled, the SMTP_APP_PASSWORD gap must be fixed first.
- **A15** (AMBER): Move gamma event enumeration to /events/keyset; re-size the poll budget against ~31k due markets; rule on whether a permanently true global_lane gap is acceptable; rotate the 425 MB log.
- **A16** (AMBER): Define what degraded means for the dossier (declared gaps above a threshold, not above zero) so the status can go green and a real failure stands out.
- **A17** (AMBER): Move consensus/data out of the dev checkout to a state home (for example ~/.openclaw/consensus) and repoint the symlink and the two plists' log paths; fix the hard-coded dev venv path in sync_from_basilic.sh.
- **A18** (AMBER): Gate the brief on the scan's completion (wait for the `<<< exit` line or a done-marker) instead of the clock; find out what took the host's network down on 09-23 and whether the INFRA-B1 assertion bundle would have caught it.
- **B2** (AMBER): Order: restore or retire the QW-1 Open WebUI lane (colima VM has failed to start since the 2026-09-04 reboot). Order: explain the nightly "prices night done, worst exit 1".
- **B3** (UNKNOWN): Order SM-B-REPRO: reproduce under observation. With the owner at the console, start `log stream --info --predicate 'process == "io.tailscale.ipn.macsys.network-extension"'` to a file, reboot once by normal login and once via Software Update's restart (auto-login then auto-lock), and record whether the tailnet IP appears before any unlock. In parallel run `tailscale bugreport` and ask Tailscale support for node Basilic's client log for 2026-09-04 05:37Z to 2026-09-20 02:22Z. Ask the owner what he clicked at 22:21 on 09-19.
- **B4** (AMBER): Order SM-B-HARDEN (sequence after SM-B-REPRO, or in parallel if the owner prefers): 1. Replace the Standalone GUI Tailscale with the open-source `tailscaled` run as a system LaunchDaemon (no user session, Keychain or Secure Enclave dependency); verify it comes up after a reboot with nobody logged in. 2. Set automatic macOS update install off on basilic; reboots become attended, done with someone able to reach the console. 3. Build INFRA-B1 as an assertion bundle that includes "utun has 100.106.84.115" and "control keepalives advancing", alerting off-host over a non-Tailscale channel. 4. Add a second remote path that does not ride on Tailscale. 5. Remove NordVPN (app, helper, Shield extension) from basilic, or at least turn off its auto-update. 6. Clear the dangling interactive login (cancel or complete it deliberately). 7. Decide the FileVault-versus-power-loss trade-off explicitly (see B5).

- **I5** (RED): SM-H1 reader half: one cohort function fed by the ledger. Fix the target quarter; only filers whose ledger state for it is filed contribute a flow; the prior must be the adjacent quarter (filed-empty prior means every position is new; awaited or NT prior means no direction). Show 'k of N reported' plus the awaited and NT names, and render the period per side in the HTML, CSV and brief.

(2) SM-H1a ledger: one table keyed (cik, period) with state filed_rows / filed_empty / nt_notice / awaited, form, accession, filed_date, first_seen, deadline with weekend and holiday roll; writer inside the nightly 13F leg reading the unfiltered feed; one-time backfill of the 230 seen accessions, the 5 orphan empties and NT history (28 GETs); tests for all four states, amendments and idempotence; scan status that names NT and awaited instead of OK. Small: one module, one table, no new dependency; needs a DB snapshot before migration. (3) SM-H1b readers: the cohort function and adjacent-quarter rule from I5 across the five call sites and three renderers, with existing tests updated. Medium, and the main risk. (4) Prove it on the Q2 window by as-of replay before Q3 arrives. (5) B2 harvest block + SIDE SWAPS on top of the cohort function: size unknown until the spec exists.
